H_{b^{\prime}_{i}}
for some
b^{\prime}_{i}\in A^{\prime}_{K}\cup B^{\prime}
. Hence
C=v_{j_{1}}b_{1}v_{j_{2}}P_{v_{j_{2}}}v^{\prime}_{j_{2}}b^{\prime}_{2}v^{\prime}_{j_{3}}P_{v_{j_{3}}}v_{j_{3}}\ldots v^{\prime}_{j_{t}}b^{\prime}_{t}v^{\prime}_{j_{1}}P_{v_{j_{1}}}v_{j_{1}}
is a cycle in
G
of length
t\ell>2\ell
.

By definition of an even template partition, there are no edges between two distinct
P_{i}
’s contained in
C
, and no edges between vertices in
A_{K}\cup B
and vertices in
A^{\prime}_{K}\cup B^{\prime}
. So the only chords
C
could contain are:

-
b_{i}b_{j}
for some distinct
i,j\in\{1,2,\ldots,t\}
of same parity, or

-
v_{j_{l}}b_{i}
for some odd
i\in\{1,2,\ldots,t\}
and
l\neq i,i+1
(where
t+1=1
), or

-
v^{\prime}_{j_{l}}b_{i}
for some even
i\in\{1,2,\ldots,t\}
and
l\neq i,i+1
(where
t+1=1
).

The first case is impossible since, by definition of a hyper cycle, the hyperedges
H_{b_{i}}
are all disjoint. The two other cases cannot occur because else
j_{l}
would be contained in the hyperedge
e_{i}
where
i\neq j_{l-1},j_{l}
, a contradiction to the definition of a hyper cycle.

So
C
is a hole which has length more than
2\ell
, a contradiction to
G\in\mathcal{C}_{2\ell}
. This proves (62).

This ends the proof of Lemma
8.8
.
∎

Notice that as a corollary of Lemma
8.8
we get that if a graph
G
in
\mathcal{C}_{2\ell}
has an even
\ell
-pretemplate partition then it is an even
\ell
-template and any of its even
\ell
-pretemplate partition is a strong even
\ell
-template partition.

We will need the following analog of Lemma
4.15
.

Lemma 8.9

Let
G=(A,B,A^{\prime},B^{\prime},I,w,w^{\prime})
be an even
\ell
-template. Two vertices
x
and
y
of
G
are twins if
and only if
x,y\in B
and
H_{x}=H_{y}
, or
x,y\in B^{\prime}
and

H^{\prime}_{x}=H^{\prime}_{y}
.

Proof.

If
x,y\in B
and
H_{x}=H_{y}
, or
x,y\in B^{\prime}
and
H^{\prime}_{x}=H^{\prime}_{y}
, then

x
and
y
are obviously twins.

We claim that for all
x\in A\cup I\cup A^{\prime}
, there exist two
vertices
a,b\in N_{G}(x)
such that
N[a]\cap N[b]=\{x\}
. If

x\in I
, choose
a
and
b
to be the only two neighbors of
x
. If

x\in A
, then let
a
be the neighbor of
x
in
I
and let
b=w
if
x\neq w
, else let
b
be any
vertex of
A\setminus\{x\}
(we remind that
w
is by definition a vertex universal in
G[A\cup B]
). In all cases, by definition of an even
template partition,
N_{G}[a]\cap N_{G}[b]=\{x\}
. The proof is similar when

x\in A^{\prime}
. So,
x
has no twin in
G
.
∎

An even
\ell
-partition
(A,B,A^{\prime},B^{\prime},I)
of an even

\ell
-template
G
is
proper
if all universal vertices of
G[A\cup B]
(respectively
G[A^{\prime}\cup B^{\prime}]
) are in
B
(respectively
B^{\prime}
).

Lemma 8.10

Every twinless even
\ell
-template
G=(A,B,A^{\prime},B^{\prime},I,w,w^{\prime})
with a proper even
\ell
-partition satisfies the following:

•

w\in B
,
w
is the unique universal vertex of
G[A\cup B]
and

G[A]
contains at least one isolated vertex
u\in A_{S}
,

•

w^{\prime}\in B^{\prime}
,
w^{\prime}
is the unique universal vertex of
G[A^{\prime}\cup B^{\prime}]
and

G[A^{\prime}]
contains at least one isolated vertex
v^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}_{S}
,

•

|A_{S}|\geq 2
.

Proof.

Since the partition is proper, all universal vertices of
G[A\cup B]
belong to
B
and since
G
is twinless,
B
contains a unique vertex which is universal in
G[A\cup B]
. Hence
w
, which is by definition of
G
a universal vertex of
G[A\cup B]
, belongs to
B
and
G[A]
contains no universal vertex since else, by the definition of an even template, this vertex would be universal in
G[A\cup B]
.
Then, by Theorem
3.4
,
G[A]
contains at least one isolated vertex say
u
. The proof for
w^{\prime}
is similar and there exists a vertex
v^{\prime}
isolated in
G[A^{\prime}]
. It remains to prove the third assertion. Assume that
|A_{S}|\leq 1
. Then, since
A
contains at least three vertices, it should be that
A_{S}=\{u\}.
Similarly we obtain that
A^{\prime}_{S}=\{v^{\prime}\}
, so
u^{\prime}=v^{\prime}.
Then by the definition of an even template
v^{\prime}
is not isolated in
G[A^{\prime}]
, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 8.11

For every integer
\ell\geq 4
, every even
\ell
-template

G
admits an even
\ell
-partition which is proper.

Proof.

Let
G=(A,B,A^{\prime},B^{\prime},I)
be a strong even
\ell
-partition of
G
. By Lemma
8.1
we know that each of
G[A\cup B]
and
G[A^{\prime}\cup B^{\prime}]
contains at least one universal vertex. If the lemma does not hold, we may assume up to symmetry, that there exists at least one vertex
w
universal in
G[A\cup B]
which is in
A
. Then from the definition of an even template it is clear that we may choose
w
in
A_{K}
.

We denote by
w_{+}
the neighbor of
w
on the principal path
P_{w}
of
G
in the partition
(A,B,A^{\prime},B^{\prime},I)
. Since
\ell\geq 4
, we have
w_{+}\in I
.
Consider now the partition
(\mathbb{A}_{K},\mathbb{A}_{S},\mathbb{B},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{K},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{S},\mathbb{B}^{\prime},\mathbb{I})
of
V(G)
where
\mathbb{A}_{K}=A_{K}\setminus\{w\}
,
\mathbb{A}_{S}=A_{S}\cup\{w_{+}\}
,
\mathbb{B}=B\cup\{w\}
,
\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{K}=A^{\prime}_{K}\setminus\{w^{\prime}\}
,
\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{S}=A^{\prime}_{S}\cup\{w^{\prime}\}
,
\mathbb{B}^{\prime}=B^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{I}=I\setminus\{w_{+}\}
. We set
\mathbb{A}=\mathbb{A}_{K}\cup\mathbb{A}_{S}
and
\mathbb{A}^{\prime}=\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{K}\cup\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{S}
.

Since
(A,B,A^{\prime},B^{\prime},I)
is an even
\ell
-partition of
G
, it is clear that
\mathbb{A}_{K}
and
\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{K}
are cliques of the same cardinality. Since the only edge in
G
between
w_{+}
and
A
is
ww_{+}
we have that
\mathbb{A}_{S}=A_{S}\cup\{w_{+}\}
is a stable set and since
w_{+}
has no neighbor in
\mathbb{A}
, any universal vertex of
G[\mathbb{A}\cup\mathbb{B}]
is in
\mathbb{B}
. As
w
is complete to
A_{S}
we get from the definition of an even template that
\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{S}=A^{\prime}_{S}\cup\{w^{\prime}\}
is a stable set of the same cardinality as
A_{S}\cup\{w_{+}\}
. Remark that
|\mathbb{A}|=|A|\geq 3
. Furthermore there exists a unique path
P_{w_{+}}=w_{+}P_{w}w^{\prime}
between
w_{+}
and
w^{\prime}
whose interior is in
\mathbb{I}
. This path has length
\ell-2
. It is also important to notice that
w
is in the interior of a path
vww_{+}
for any
v\in A\setminus\{w\}
. Every other vertex of
\mathbb{B}
is in the interior of a path of
G[\mathbb{A}\cup\mathbb{B}]
with both ends in
\mathbb{A}
, path which is the same as in the initial partition since obviously this path did not contain
w
. With all these observations it is easy to conclude that
\mathbb{P}=(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{B},\mathbb{A}^{\prime},\mathbb{B}^{\prime},\mathbb{I})
fulfills all conditions to be a pretemplate partition of
G
. By Lemma
8.7
we know that
G\in\mathcal{C}_{2\ell}
. So by Lemma
8.8
and the fact that
w_{+}
is isolated in
G[\mathbb{A}]
,
\mathbb{P}
is an even
\ell
-partition of
G
such that no vertex of
\mathbb{A}
is universal in
G[\mathbb{A}\cup\mathbb{B}]
.

If
\mathbb{A}^{\prime}
contains no universal vertex of
G[\mathbb{A}^{\prime}\cup\mathbb{B}^{\prime}]
then
\mathbb{P}
is a proper even
\ell
-partition of
G
, else a proof similar to the one above allows to obtain from
\mathbb{P}
a proper even
\ell
-partition of
G
.
∎

Lemma 8.12

For every integer
\ell\geq 4
, every even
\ell
-template
G

contains a prism or a theta.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.11
,
G
admits a proper even
\ell
-partition
(A,B,A^{\prime},B^{\prime})
of
G
and by definition of an even template,
G
contains three vertices
v_{1},v_{2}
and
v_{3}
in
A
and the corresponding
vertices
v^{\prime}_{1},v^{\prime}_{2}
and
v^{\prime}_{3}
in
A^{\prime}
. Considering all kinds of repartition of
v_{1},v_{2}
and
v_{3}
in
A_{K}
and
A_{S}
, all kinds of attachment between them respecting the rules of the partition and the fact that
B
(respectively
B^{\prime}
) contains a vertex universal in
G[A_{K}\cup A_{S}\cup B]
(respectively
G[A^{\prime}_{K}\cup A^{\prime}_{S}\cup B^{\prime}]
) it is easy to verify that in each case we obtain either a prism or a theta.
∎

8.4
Blowups and holes

We may define flat, solid and optional edges in even
\ell
-template partitions exactly as in the case of odd
\ell
-template partitions (see subsection
5.1
). In the following we will also use the notion of blowup and preblowup of an even template
\ell
-partition with the same definition as for an odd
\ell
-template partition (see subsections
5.2
and
5.3
).

The following lemma can be proved similarly as Lemma
5.4
.

Lemma 8.13

A hole
C
in a blowup of a twinless even
\ell
-template contains at
most one vertex in each blown up clique.

We remark that Lemmas
5.1
,
5.2
and
5.3
are valid for any odd or even template as they rely only on the definition of solid and optional edges. Hence, the following lemma has the same proof as Lemma
5.5
of the odd case, except that we use Lemma
8.7
instead of Lemma
4.11
, and Lemma
8.13
instead of Lemma
5.4
.

Lemma 8.14

In a blowup
G^{*}
of a twinless even
\ell
-template
G
, every hole
has length
2\ell
.

Recall that to blowup (resp. preblowup) a template, one needs to
first fix an
\ell
-partition. If this partition is proper, the
blowup (resp. preblowup) is
proper
.
Recall also that when
G^{*}
is a preblowup of a template

G
, the
domination score
of
G
w.r.t.
G^{*}
is defined as (where
N
refers to the neighborhood in
G^{*}
):

s(G,G^{*})=\sum_{x\in A\cup A^{\prime}\cup I}\left|\left\{x^{*}\in K_{x}:N[x^{*}]\subseteq N[x]\right\}\right|

Lemma 8.15

Let
\ell\geq 3
and let
G^{*}
be a proper preblowup of an even
\ell
-template
with
k\geq 3
principal paths. If
G^{*}\in\mathcal{C}_{2\ell}
,
then
G^{*}
is a proper blowup of a twinless even
\ell
-template
G

with
k
principal paths (in particular,
G
is an induced subgraph
of
G^{*}
).

Proof.

Among all the induced subgraphs of
G^{*}
that are even

\ell
-templates and for which
G^{*}
is a proper preblowup,
we suppose that
G
is one that maximizes
s(G,G^{*})
. We denote
by
(A,B,A^{\prime},B^{\prime},I,w,w^{\prime})
the proper
\ell
-partition of
G
that
is used for its preblowup and by
(A^{*},B^{*},A^{\prime*},B^{\prime*},I^{*})
the
corresponding partition of the vertices of
G^{*}
.

(
63)
There exist vertices
w^{*}\in B^{*}
and
w^{\prime*}\in B^{\prime*}
that are complete to
respectively
A^{*}
and
A^{\prime*}.

Since the partition is proper,
w\in B
and then by condition (
b1
) of a preblowup, there
exists
w^{*}\in B^{*}
that is complete to
A^{*}
.

The proof of the statement about
w^{\prime*}
is similar.
This proves (63).

(
64)
For every principal path
P_{u}=u\dots u^{\prime}
of
G
and
u^{*}\in K_{u}
,
there exists in
G^{*}
a path
P_{u^{*}}

from
u^{*}
to some
{u}^{\prime*}\in K_{u^{\prime}}
whose interior is in

\bigcup_{x\in I\cap V(P_{u})}K_{x}
and whose length is equal to the length of
P_{u}
. Moreover, the interior of
P_{u^{*}}
is
anticomplete to
V(G^{*})\setminus\bigcup_{v\in V(P_{u})}K_{v}
.

The existence of a path of same length as
P_{u}
from
u^{*}
to some
u^{\prime*}\in K_{u^{\prime}}
whose
interior is in
\bigcup_{x\in I\cap V(P)}K_{x}
follows from conditions (
a2
), (
i
) and (
i1
),
of preblowup. The statement about its interior follows from
conditions (
a
), (
b
) and (
i
) of
preblowup.
This proves (64).

(
65)
For all
u,v\in A
such that
uv\notin E(G)
,
K_{u}
is anticomplete
to
K_{v}
. A similar statement holds for
A^{\prime}
.

Suppose that there exist
u^{*}\in K_{u}
and
v^{*}\in K_{v}
such that

u^{*}v^{*}\in E(G^{*})
. By condition (
a1
) of preblowup,

u\neq u^{*}
and
v\neq v^{*}
.

Since
uv\notin E(G)
then at least one of
u,v
is in
A_{S}
, say
v\in A_{S}
.

Consider first the case where
u\in A_{K}.

The principal paths
P_{u}=u\dots u^{\prime}
and

P_{v}=v\dots v^{\prime}
have length respectively
\ell-1
and
\ell-2
. Denote by
u^{+}
the neighbor
of
u
in
P_{u}
and by
v^{+}
the neighbor of
v
in
P_{v}
. By
property (
e
) of an even template,

u^{\prime}v^{\prime}\in E(G)
. Hence

uP_{u}u^{\prime}v^{\prime}P_{v}vv^{*}u^{*}u
is a cycle
C
of length
2\ell+1
. By
conditions (
a
) and (
a1
) of preblowup, the only
possible chords in
C
are
u^{*}u^{+}
and
v^{*}v^{+}
. Assume that
u^{*}u^{+}\in E(G^{*})
.
Let
P_{v^{*}}
be a path of length
\ell-2
from
v^{*}
to
v^{\prime*}

as defined in (
8.4
). Since
v^{\prime*}\in K_{v^{\prime}}
and
by (
a1
) of the preblowup applied to
A^{\prime}
,
v^{\prime*}u^{\prime}\in E(G^{*})
. So

v^{*}P_{v^{*}}v^{\prime*}u^{\prime}P_{u}u^{+}u^{*}v^{*}
is a hole of length
2\ell-1
, a
contradiction. Hence
v^{+}v^{*}
should be a chord of
C
. Let then
P_{u^{*}}
be a path of length
\ell-1
from
u^{*}
to
u^{\prime*}
as defined in (
8.4
). Since
u^{\prime*}\in K_{u^{\prime}}
and
by (
a1
) of the preblowup applied to
A^{\prime}
,
u^{\prime*}v^{\prime}\in E(G^{*})
. So

u^{*}P_{u^{*}}u^{\prime*}v^{\prime}P_{v}v^{+}v^{*}u^{*}
is a hole of length
2\ell-1
, a
contradiction again.

It remains to consider the case where both
u
and
v
are in
A_{S}
. Let
P_{u}=u\dots u^{\prime}
and
P_{v}=v\dots v^{\prime}
be principal paths of length
\ell-2
. Denote by
u^{+}
the neighbor
of
u
in
P_{u}
and by
v^{+}
the neighbor of
v
in
P_{v}
. By property (
e
) of an even template,

u^{\prime}v^{\prime}\notin E(G)
, hence

uP_{u}u^{\prime}w^{\prime*}v^{\prime}P_{v}vv^{*}u^{*}u
is a cycle
C
of length
2\ell+1
. By conditions (
a
) and (
a1
) of preblowup, the only possible chords in
C
are
u^{+}u^{*}
and
v^{+}v^{*}
.

Assume without loss of generality that
u^{+}u^{*}\in E(G^{*})
. Let
P_{v^{*}}
be a path of length
\ell-2
from
v^{*}
to
v^{\prime*}

as defined in (
8.4
). Since
v^{\prime*}\in K_{v^{\prime}}
and
by (
a1
) applied to
A^{\prime}
,
v^{\prime*}u^{\prime}\in E(G^{*})
. So

v^{*}P_{v^{*}}v^{\prime*}w^{\prime*}u^{\prime}P_{u}u^{+}u^{*}v^{*}
is a hole of length
2\ell-1
, a
contradiction.

The result for
A^{\prime}
holds symmetrically.
This proves (65).

(
66)
For all
u,v\in A
such that
uv\in E(G)
,
K_{u}
is complete to

K_{v}
. A similar statement holds for
A^{\prime}
.

Suppose that there exist
u^{*}\in K_{u}
and
v^{*}\in K_{v}
such that

u^{*}v^{*}\notin E(G^{*})
. Let
P_{u^{*}}=u^{*}\dots u^{\prime*}
and

P_{v^{*}}=v^{*}\dots v^{\prime*}
be defined as
in (
8.4
). Observe that
u^{\prime*}\in K_{u^{\prime}}
and

v^{\prime*}\in K_{v^{\prime}}
.

Since
uv\in E(G)
then at least one of
u,v
is in
A_{K}
, say
u\in A_{K}
.

Consider first the case where
v\in A_{K}
too. Then both
P_{u^{*}}
and
P_{v^{*}}
have length
\ell-1
.
If
u^{\prime*}v^{\prime*}\in E(G^{*})
then
u^{*}P_{u^{*}}u^{\prime*}v^{\prime*}P_{v^{*}}v^{*}w^{*}u^{*}
is a hole of length
2\ell+1
and else
u^{*}P_{u^{*}}u^{\prime*}w^{\prime*}v^{\prime*}P_{v^{*}}v^{*}w^{*}u^{*}
is a hole of length
2\ell+2
, so it should be that
v\in A_{S}
. Then
P_{u^{*}}
and
P_{v^{*}}
have length respectively
\ell-1
and
\ell-2
. Moreover by property (
e
) of an even template,

u^{\prime}v^{\prime}\notin E(G^{*})
, so by (
8.4
)
u^{\prime*}v^{\prime*}\notin E(G^{*})
. Now
u^{*}P_{u^{*}}u^{\prime*}w^{\prime*}v^{\prime*}P_{v^{*}}v^{*}w^{*}u^{*}
is a hole of length
2\ell+1
, a contradiction again.

This proves (66).

(
67)
For all
u\in I
and
u_{1},u_{2}\in K_{u}
, either

N[u_{1}]\subseteq N[u_{2}]
or
N[u_{2}]\subseteq N[u_{1}]
.

Otherwise, there exist
x^{*}_{1}\in N[u_{1}]\setminus N[u_{2}]
and

x^{*}_{2}\in N[u_{2}]\setminus N[u_{1}]
. Note that

x^{*}_{1}x^{*}_{2}\notin E(G^{*})
for otherwise,

\{x^{*}_{1},x^{*}_{2},u_{1},u_{2}\}
induces a
C_{4}
. It follows that
x^{*}_{1}
and
x^{*}_{2}

belong respectively to distinct cliques
K_{x_{1}}
and
K_{x_{2}}
,
where
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
are the two neighbors of
u

along some principal path
P_{v}=v\dots v^{\prime}
of
G
. Because of

x^{*}_{1}
,
x^{*}_{2}
and condition (
i1
) of
preblowup, there exists a path
P^{*}
from some

v^{*}\in K_{v}
to some
v^{\prime*}\in K_{v^{\prime}}
whose interior is in

\bigcup_{x\in I\cap V(P_{v})}K_{x}
which contains
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
and has a length equal to the length of
P_{v}
plus one.

Assume first that
v\in A_{K}
. Then
P^{*}
has length
\ell
.
By Lemma
8.10
, since
(A,B,A^{\prime},B^{\prime},I,w,w^{\prime})
is a proper
\ell
-partition of
G
,
A_{S}
is not empty. Let
q\neq v
be a vertex in
A_{S}
and
P_{q}=q\dots q^{\prime}
be the principal path of length
\ell-2
joining
q
and
q^{\prime}
in
G
.
Up to symmetry we may assume that
qv\notin E(G)
and
q^{\prime}v^{\prime}\in E(G)
. Now, by
condition (
i
) of preblowup
and (
5.3
),
P^{*}
,
P_{q}
and
w^{*}
form a hole of length

2\ell+1
, a contradiction.

So
v
should be in
A_{S}
and
P^{*}
has length
\ell-1
. Since the
\ell
-partition of
G
is proper, there exists a vertex
q\in A_{S}
distinct from
v
,
P_{q}
has length
\ell-2
and there exists no edge between
P^{*}
and
P_{q}
. Then
P^{*},P_{q},w^{*}
and
w^{\prime*}
induce a hole of length
2\ell+1
, a contradiction again.
This proves (67).

(
68)
For all
u\in I
and
u^{*}\in K_{u}
,

N[u^{*}]\subseteq N[u]
.

Otherwise, by (
8.4
), there exists a vertex

u^{*}\in K_{u}
such that
N[u]\subsetneq N[u^{*}]
. Hence

(V(G)\setminus\{u\})\cup\{u^{*}\}
induces a subgraph
G_{0}
of
G^{*}

and it is easy to verify that
G^{*}
is a preblowup of
G_{0}
. This
contradicts the maximality of
s(G,G^{*})
.
This proves (68).

By (
8.4
), for every
u\in I
, the clique
K_{u}
can be
linearly ordered by the inclusion of the neighborhoods as

u_{1},\dots,u_{k_{u}}
with

by (

) (so,

1\leq i\leq j\leq k_{u}
,

). From

) of the preblowup it also follows that, in
G^{*}
,

is complete to the cliques
associated to its two neighbors in
G
.

(
69)
For every
u\in A
and
u_{1},u_{2}\in K_{u}
, either

N[u_{1}]\subseteq N[u_{2}]
or
N[u_{2}]\subseteq N[u_{1}]
. A similar statement holds for
A^{\prime}
.

Otherwise, there exist
x_{1}\in N[u_{1}]\setminus N[u_{2}]
and

x_{2}\in N[u_{2}]\setminus N[u_{1}]
. Note that
x_{1}x_{2}\notin E(G^{*})

for otherwise,
\{x_{1},x_{2},u_{1},u_{2}\}
induces a
C_{4}
.

Observe first that by (
5.3
) and (

),
N_{A^{*}}[u_{1}]=N_{A^{*}}[u_{2}]
. Hence

a
) of preblowup,
x_{1},x_{2}\in B^{*}\cup K_{u^{+}}
where
u^{+}
is the neighbor of
u
in the principal path that contains
u
. Without loss of
generality and since
K_{u^{+}}
is a clique,
x_{1}\in B^{*}
.

By condition (
b2
) of preblowup, there exist non-adjacent
a,b\in A

such that

has neighbors

and
b^{*}\in K_{b}
, and by (

)

Note that

because

. If

u_{2}
is complete to
\{a^{*},b^{*}\}
, then

, a contradiction. So, up to symmetry

u_{2}a^{*}\notin E(G)
. So,
a^{*}\notin K_{u}
and

) and (

),
a^{*}u_{1}\notin E(G^{*})
.
Observe

for otherwise

.

Suppose that
x_{2}\in B^{*}
. As above, we can show that

has a neighbor

that is anticomplete to

.
Let

and

). To avoid a hole

of length

, we have

.
Since the lengths of

and

, depending whether

or

is a hole of length

, a contradiction.
So

. Using condition (

) of

from

to some

), which contains

. Now

is a hole of length

, a contradiction.

The result for

holds symmetrically.

(
70)
For all
u\in A
and

,

. A

.

Otherwise, by (

By (

), for every

, the clique

, and by (

(so,

).

(
71)
If

is an edge of
G[B^{*}]
, then either

or

.

Otherwise, there exist

. Note that

. Let

, a contradiction.

(

For every

, there exist non-adjacent

such

.

This follows from condition (

) of

).

Two vertices

in

are

if

.

(
73)
If

and

are equivalent vertices of

, then

.

If

, then

.
This proves (73).

Vertices of
B^{*}
are partitioned into equivalence
classes. By (
8.4
), each equivalence class is a clique

X
, and by (
8.4
), vertices of
X
can be linearly
ordered according to the inclusion of neighborhoods in
A^{*}
. In each such a

we choose a vertex
x
maximal for the order and call

the set of these maximal vertices. For every

, we
denote by

the clique of
B^{*}
of all vertices equivalent to

x
. Remind that
w^{*}\in B^{*}
:
w^{*}
is a maximal vertex of its
clique. Hence, we can set
w^{*}\in B_{1}
.

So, for every
u\in B_{1}
, the clique
K_{u}
can be linearly
ordered by the inclusion of the neighborhod in
A^{*}
as

u_{1},\dots,u_{k_{u}}
with
u=u_{k_{u}}
(so, for

1\leq i\leq j\leq k_{u}
,
N_{A^{*}}(u_{i})\subseteq N_{A^{*}}(u_{j})
).

Statements similar to (
8.4
), (
8.4
), (
8.4
)
hold for
B^{\prime*}
and we define

as well.

We set
G_{1}=G^{*}[A\cup B_{1}\cup A^{\prime}\cup B^{\prime}_{1}\cup I]
and claim that

\ell
-pretemplate partition of

.
Since

,

3.
), (
4.
) (

), (

) and (

)
hold. Adding the fact that

by condition (

) of preblowup, condition (

) for
a pretemplate holds and symmetrically also condition
(
2.
). Now condition (

) holds because

and

are universal in respectively

and

. Hence, by Lemma

\ell
-template. It is twinless by

. We also notice that by construction

(respectively

) belongs to

and is universal in

(respectively

). Since

is a proper

-partition of

, there exist isolated vertices in

and
