. Hence

(A,B_{1},A^{\prime},B^{\prime}_{1},I,w^{*},w^{\prime*})
is a proper even
\ell
-partition of

G_{1}
.

We now prove that

G^{*}
is a proper blowup of
G_{1}
.

By the definition of a preblowup and by (
8.4
), for all
u\in V(G_{1})
,
K_{u}
is a clique and
V(G^{*})=\bigcup_{u\in V(G_{1})}K_{u}

(
74)
If
u,v\in V(G_{1})
and
uv\notin E(G_{1})
, then
K_{u}
is anticomplete to
K_{v}
.

If
u\in I
or

v\in I
, the conclusion follows directly from
condition (
i
) of preblowup. So we may assume up to
symmetry that
u\in A\cup B_{1}
. By conditions (
a
)
and (
b
) of preblowup, we may assume
v\in A\cup B_{1}
.
If
u,v\in A
, then the result follows from
(
5.3
), so we may assume that
v\in B_{1}
.

Now suppose for a contradiction that there exist
u^{*}\in K_{u}
and

v^{*}\in K_{v}
such that
u^{*}v^{*}\in E(G_{1})
. By the choice of vertices
in
B_{1}
, for all
v^{*}\in K_{v}
,
N[v^{*}]\subseteq N[v]
. So

u^{*}v\in E(G_{1})
. For the same reason or by (
8.4
), for
all
u^{*}\in K_{u}
,
N[u^{*}]\subseteq N[u]
. Hence
uv\in E(G_{1})
, a
contradiction.
This proves (74).

(
75)
If
uv
is a solid edge of
G_{1}
then
K_{u}
is complete to
K_{v}
.

Otherwise, let
u^{*}\in K_{u}
and
v^{*}\in K_{v}
such that
u^{*}v^{*}\notin E(G)
. Since
uv
is a solid edge, up to symmetry,
u,v\in A
or

u,v\in B_{1}
or
u\in A
,
v\in B_{1}
and in this last case
u
is not an isolated vertex
of
G[H_{v}]
.

By (
8.4
) the case where
u
and
v
are in
A

cannot happen. Assume then that

v\in B_{1}
. By Lemma
8.3
, there exist

a,b\in H_{v}
(and hence in
A
) that are not adjacent. Assume
that
u
is also in
B_{1}
. Since
u
and
v
are adjacent, by (
8.4
) we may
assume without loss of generality that
H_{v}\subseteq H_{u}
and so

a
and
b
belong to
H_{u}
too. Then, by the definition of
K_{u}

and
K_{v}
, we get a
C_{4}
induced by
\{u^{*},v^{*},a,b\}
, a
contradiction.

So
u
should be in
A,
and to avoid a
C_{4}

induced by
\{u^{*},v^{*},a,b\}
,
u^{*}
should be non-adjacent to at
least one of
a
and
b
, say
a
. In particular,
a\neq u
.
Then, by (
8.4
),
ua\notin E(G_{1})
. So
u
does
not belong to
N(H_{v})
and since
uv
is an edge of
G_{1}
, we get
that
u\in H_{v}
. Since
uv
is solid,
u
has at least one neighbor in

H_{v}
, and we know that
u
is not adjacent to at least one vertex in
H_{v}
(namely
a
). Hence, as
H_{v}
is anticonnected, there exist non-adjacent
vertices
c,d\in H_{v}
such that
uc\notin E(G_{1})
and

ud\in E(G_{1})
. Now
u^{*}P_{u^{*}}u^{\prime*}c^{\prime}P_{c}cv^{*}du^{*}
or
u^{*}P_{u^{*}}u^{\prime*}w^{\prime*}c^{\prime}P_{c}cv^{*}du^{*}
is a hole of
length
2\ell+1
, a contradiction again.

This proves (75).

(
76)
For all
u\in V(G_{1})
and
1\leq i\leq j\leq k_{u}
,

N[u_{i}]\subseteq N[u_{j}]
.

The result follows from how vertices are ordered after the proof of
(
8.4
)
(vertices in
I
), (
8.4
) (vertices in
A
or
A^{\prime}
) and
(
8.4
) (vertices in
B_{1}
or
B^{\prime}_{1}
) and from (
8.4
).
This proves (76).

(
77) If
uv
is a flat edge of
G_{1}
, then
u
is
complete to
K_{v}
and
v
is complete to
K_{u}
.

By definition of a flat edge, either
u
and
v
are in
I
or one
is in
I
and the other is in
A
or in
A^{\prime}
. The result follows
from (
8.4
), (
8.4
), and
conditions (
a2
) (applied to
A
or
A^{\prime}
)
and (
i1
) of the preblowup.
This proves (77).

(
78)
If
ux
is an optional edge of
G_{1}
with
u\in A

and
x\in B_{1}
(resp.
u\in A^{\prime}
and
x\in B_{1}^{\prime}
), then
u
is
complete to
K_{x}
.

The result follows from the definition of
K_{x}
when
x\in B_{1}
.
This proves (78).

(
79)
If
ux
and
uy
are optional edges with
u\in A
,
x,y\in B_{1}

and
H_{y}\subsetneq H_{x}
(resp.
u\in A^{\prime}
,
x,y\in B_{1}^{\prime}
and

H^{\prime}_{y}\subsetneq H^{\prime}_{x}
), then every vertex of
K_{u}
with a neighbor
in
K_{y}
is complete to
K_{x}
.

Otherwise, let
u^{*}
be a vertex in
K_{u}
that has a neighbor

y^{*}
in
K_{y}
and a non-neighbor
x^{*}
in
K_{x}
. Since

H_{x}
and
H_{y}
are not disjoint,
xy
is a solid edge of
G_{1}
and
by (
8.4
),
x^{*}y^{*}\in E(G_{1})
.

Since
x
and
y
are not equivalent, there exists a vertex
a
such that
a\in N_{A}(y)\setminus N_{A}(x)
or
a\in N_{A}(x)\setminus N_{A}(y)
. In the first case, by definition of a template,
a\in A\setminus N_{A}[H_{x}]
. Then since
H_{y}\subsetneq H_{x}
and
H_{x}
is a module of
A
we get that
a
is anticomplete to
H_{x}
and hence to
H_{y}
. So
a\notin N_{A}(y)
, a contradiction; we may then conclude that
a\in N_{A}(x)\setminus N_{A}(y)

By definition of the cliques in
B
,
x^{*}a\in E(G^{*})
and
y^{*}a\notin E(G^{*})
. Therefore, to avoid a
C_{4}
induced by
\{x^{*},y^{*},u^{*},a\}
, it should be that
u^{*}a\notin E(G^{*})
.

Now
aP_{a}a^{\prime}u^{\prime*}P_{u^{*}}u^{*}y^{*}x^{*}a
or
aP_{a}a^{\prime}w^{\prime*}u^{\prime*}P_{u^{*}}u^{*}y^{*}x^{*}a
is a hole of length
2\ell+1
a
contradiction.
This proves (79).

(
80)
w^{*}
(resp.
w^{\prime*}
) is a universal vertex of

G^{*}[\bigcup_{u\in A\cup B_{1}}K_{u}]
(resp.
G^{*}[\bigcup_{u\in A^{\prime}\cup B_{1}^{\prime}}K_{u}]
).

By (
5.3
),
w^{*}
is complete to
A^{*}
and
so to
\bigcup_{u\in A}K_{u}
. Furthermore, from the definition of

G_{1}
we know that
w^{*}
is complete to
B_{1}\setminus\{w^{*}\}
. Since all edges between vertices in
B_{1}
are solid,
by (
8.4
),
w^{*}
is complete to

B^{*}\setminus\{w^{*}\}
. Hence
w^{*}
is a universal
vertex of
G^{*}[\bigcup_{u\in A\cup B}K_{u}]
. The proof for
w^{\prime*}
is
symmetric.
This proves (80).

From all the claims above,
G^{*}
satisfies all conditions to be a
proper blowup of
G_{1}
.
∎

9
Graphs in
\mathcal{C}_{2\ell}
that contain a theta or a prism

The goal of this section is to prove the following.

Lemma 9.1

Let
\ell\geq 4
be an integer. If
G
is a graph in

\mathcal{C}_{2\ell}
and
G
contains a theta or a prism, then one of the
following holds:

(a)

G
is a proper blowup of a twinless even

\ell
-template;

(b)

G
has a universal vertex;

(c)

G
has a clique cutset.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of
Lemma
9.1
. So from here on,
\ell\geq 4
is an integer and
G

is graph in
\mathcal{C}_{2\ell}
that contains a theta
\Theta
or a prism
\Sigma
.
By Lemma
3.10
, the three paths of
\Theta
have
length
\ell
and those of
\Sigma
have length
\ell-1
. By Lemma
8.2
,
\Theta
and
\Sigma
are even
\ell
-templates. Hence, we may define an integer
k
and a sequence

F_{0},F_{1},F_{2}
of induced subgraphs of
G
as follows.

•

k
is the maximum integer such that
G
contains
an even
\ell
-template with
k
principal paths. Observe that by
Lemma
8.9
,
G
in fact contains a twinless even
\ell
-template with

k
principal paths, because twins can be eliminated from templates
by deleting hyperedges with equal vertex-set while there are some.

•

In
G
, pick a proper blowup
F_{1}
of a twinless even

\ell
-template
F_{0}
with
k
principal paths. Note that
F_{0}

exists and the proper
\ell
-partition needed for the proper blowup
exists by Lemma
8.11
.

•

Suppose that
F_{0}
and
F_{1}
are chosen subject to the
maximality of the vertex-set of
F_{1}
(in the sense of
inclusion). Note that possibly
F_{0}
is not a maximal template in
the sense of inclusion, it can be that a smaller template leads to a
bigger blowup (but
F_{0}
has
k
principal paths).

•

F_{2}
is obtained from
F_{1}
by adding all vertices of

G\setminus F_{1}
that are complete to
F_{1}
.

Lemma 9.2

V(F_{2})\setminus V(F_{1})
is a (possibly empty) clique that is
complete to
F_{1}
.

Proof.

Otherwise,
G
contains a
C_{4}
.
∎

We now introduce some notation. We denote by
(A,B,A^{\prime},B^{\prime},I,w,w^{\prime})
the
twinless proper even
\ell
-partition that is used to blow up
F_{0}
. We have
A=A_{K}\cup A_{S}
and
A^{\prime}=A^{\prime}_{K}\cup A^{\prime}_{S}
.
When
u
is a vertex
of
F_{0}
, we denote by
K_{u}
the clique of
F_{1}
that is blown up from

u
. We set
A_{K}^{*}=\bigcup_{u\in A_{K}}K_{u}
. We use a similar notation
A_{S}^{*}
,
{A^{\prime}_{K}}^{*}
,
{A^{\prime}_{S}}^{*}
,

B^{*}
,
B^{\prime*}
,
I^{*}
,
A^{*}
and
A^{\prime*}
.

9.1
Technical lemmas

We now prove lemmas that sum up several structural properties of
G
.

Lemma 9.3

If
u\in A\cup A^{\prime}\cup I\cup\{w,w^{\prime}\}
and
v\in N_{V(F_{0})}(u)
,
then
u
is complete to
K_{v}
.

Proof.

We prove this lemma using the conditions from the definition of
blowups. If
u\in\{w,w^{\prime}\}
, then the result follows from
condition (
h
). If
u\in A\cup A^{\prime}
, then the conclusion
follows from conditions (
d
), (
e
) and (
f
). If
u\in I
, then the conclusion follows from
condition (
e
).
∎

Very often, Lemma
9.3
will be used in the
following way. Suppose there exists a principal path
P=u\dots u^{\prime}
of

F_{0}
. Suppose there exists a vertex
x
of
P
and
x^{*}\in K_{x}
.
Then by Lemma
9.3
and
condition (
c
) of blowups,

\{x^{*}\}\cup(V(P)\setminus\{x\})
induces a path of
F_{1}
. If
y\neq x

is a vertex of
P
and
y^{*}\in K_{y}
, then

\{x^{*},y^{*}\}\cup(V(P)\setminus\{x,y\})
might fail to induce a path of

F_{1}
, because it is possible that
xy\in E(G)
while

x^{*}y^{*}\notin E(G)
. But under the assumption that
x^{*}y^{*}\in E(G)

or
xy\notin E(G)
, we do have that

\{x^{*},y^{*}\}\cup(V(P)\setminus\{x,y\})
induces a path of

F_{1}
. Several variant of this situation will appear soon and we will
simply justify them by refering to
Lemma
9.3
.

When
u
is a vertex in
A
, we denote by
P_{u}
the unique principal
path of
F_{0}
that contains
u
. Its end in
A^{\prime}
is then denoted by

u^{\prime}
. We denote by
u^{+}
the neighbor of
u
in
P_{u}
. We denote by

u^{++}
the neighbor of
u^{+}
in
P_{u}\setminus u
. Note that
u^{+}\in I

and
u^{++}\in I\cup A^{\prime}
(
u^{++}\in A^{\prime}
if and only if
\ell=4
and
u\in A_{S}
).

For any distinct
u,v\in A
, from the definition of even templates,
exactly one of

V(P_{u})\cup V(P_{v})
or
V(P_{u})\cup V(P_{v})\cup\{w\}
or

V(P_{u})\cup V(P_{v})\cup\{w^{\prime}\}
or

V(P_{u})\cup V(P_{v})\cup\{w,w^{\prime}\}
induces a hole that is denoted by

C_{u,v}
. Such a hole is called a
principal hole
.

So, there are three kinds of principal holes: those that contain
exactly one of
w
and
w^{\prime}
, those that contain none of
w
and
w^{\prime}
and those that contain both
w
and
w^{\prime}
. Recall that by
Lemma
8.7
, some holes of a template contain two
principal paths plus one or two extra vertices, but it may fail to be a
principal hole (because the extra vertices may fail to be
w
or
w^{\prime}
). Though
we do not use this information formally, it is worth noting that by
Lemma
9.3
, when
C
is a principal hole,

\bigcup_{v\in V(C)}K_{v}
induces a ring. But when
C
is a non-principal
hole, it may happen that
\bigcup_{v\in V(C)}K_{v}
does not induce a
ring (because there might be in
C
an optional edge
uv
with

u\in A
and
v\in B
, and after the blowup process, there might be that no
vertex in
K_{v}
is complete to
K_{u}
).

Lemma 9.4

If
u\in V(F_{0})
and
u^{*}\in K_{u}
, then
u^{*}
has two
neighbors in
V(F_{0})\setminus K_{u}
that are not adjacent.

Proof.

If
u\in I
, then let
P
be the principal path that contains
u
. By
Lemma
9.3
,
u^{*}
is adjacent to the two
neighbors of
u
in
P
.

If
u\in A\cup A^{\prime}
, say
u\in A
up to symmetry, then we claim that

u
has a neighbor
z
in
A\cup B
. This is clear if
u
is not
isolated in
A
and otherwise we set
z=w
. By
Lemma
9.3
,
z
and
u^{+}
are
non-adjacent neighbors of
u^{*}
.

If
u\in B
, then by the definition of a template,
H_{u}
contains two non adjacent vertices
a
and
b
that are neighbors of
u
.
By Lemma
9.3
,
a
and
b
are
both adjacent to
u^{*}
.
∎

Lemma 9.5

If
K
is a clique of
F_{0}
,
K^{*}=\bigcup_{v\in K}K_{v}
and
D
is a
connected induced subgraph of
G\setminus F_{2}
such that

N_{V(F_{1})}(D)\subseteq K^{*}
, then
N_{V(F_{1})}(D)
is a clique.

Proof.

For suppose not. This means that there exists

u^{*},v^{*}\in K^{*}
and
x_{u},x_{v}\in D
such that

u^{*}v^{*}\notin E(G)
and
x_{u}u^{*},x_{v}v^{*}\in E(G)
(possibly

x_{u}=x_{v}
). Since
D
is connected, there exists a path
P
in
D

from
x_{u}
to
x_{v}
. Suppose that
u^{*}
,
x_{u}
,
v^{*}
,
x_{v}
and

P
are chosen subject to the minimality of
P
. It follows that

u^{*}x_{u}Px_{v}v^{*}
is a path, and recall that by assumption
its interior is anticomplete to
F_{1}\setminus K^{*}
.

Since
u^{*}v^{*}\notin E(G)
,
u^{*}
and
v^{*}
are in different blown-up
cliques. Denote by
K_{u}
and
K_{v}
the blown-up cliques such that

u^{*}\in K_{u}
and
v^{*}\in K_{v}
. By hypothesis,
u,v\in K
and so

uv\in E(G)
. Since
u^{*}v^{*}\notin E(G)
, by
condition (
d
) of blowups,
uv
is not a solid edge of

G
.

If
uv
is a flat edge of
F_{0}
, then
uv
is an edge of a
principal path and we know that this principal path belongs to a
principal hole
C
. Note that apart from
u
and

v
, no vertex of
C
is in
K
since
K
is a clique. By
Lemma
9.3
, in
G
,

(\{u^{*},v^{*}\})\cup V(C))\setminus\{u,v\}
induces a path
Q
of length

2\ell-1
. So
P
and
Q
form a hole of length at least
2\ell+1
,
a contradiction.

If
uv
is an optional edge of
F_{0}
, say with
u\in A
and

v\in B
, then
u\in H_{v}
, and there exists
a
in
H_{v}
such that

au\notin E(F_{0})
. Therefore,
P_{u}
,
P_{a}
,
v
and possibly
w^{\prime}
form a hole

C^{*}
. By condition (
f
) of blowups (if
va
is optional),
or by condition (
d
) (if
va
is solid),
a
is complete
to
K_{v}
. By Lemma
9.3
it follows that

(\{u^{*},v^{*}\})\cup V(C^{*}))\setminus\{u,v\}
induces a path
Q
of length

2\ell-1
. So
P
and
Q
form a hole of length at least
2\ell+1
,
a contradiction again.
∎

Lemma 9.6

If
x\in V(G)\setminus V(F_{2})
and
C
is a principal hole of
F_{0}
, then
x

is minor w.r.t.
C
.

(We remind that
x
is minor w.r.t.
C
if the neighborhood of
x
in
C
is included in a
3
-vertex path of
C
.)

Proof.

Otherwise let
C=C_{u,v}
for some
u,v\in A
such that

x
is major w.r.t.
C
. By Lemma
3.10
,
x
and
C
form a universal wheel.

(
81)

x
is complete to all principal paths.

We know already that
x
is complete to
P_{u}
and
P_{v}
.
Let
P_{t}=t\dots t^{\prime}
be a
principal path where
t\neq u,v
.
If one of
u,v
, say
u
, is in
A_{K}
then
P_{u}
contains at least
4
vertices, so
x
is major w.r.t.
C_{u,t}
and as above we may conclude that
x
and
C_{u,t}
form a universal wheel. Consider now the case where both
u
and
v
are in
A_{S}
. Then
C
contains
w
and
w^{\prime}
and
C_{u,t}
contains at least one of
w,w^{\prime}
. So
x
is is adjacent to at least
4
vertices of
C_{u,t}
and we may again conclude that
x
is complete to
P_{t}
.
This proves (81).

(
82)

x
is complete to
B\cup B^{\prime}
.

Let
y\in B\cup B^{\prime}
. By definition of a
template,
y
has two neighbors
a
and
b
, both in
A
or both in

A^{\prime}
, that are non-adjacent. Therefore
a
,
b
,
y
and
x
form a

C_{4}
, unless
x
is adjacent to
y
.
This proves (82).

By (
9.1
) and (
9.1
),
x
is complete to
I\cup A\cup A^{\prime}\cup B\cup B^{\prime}=V(F_{0})
.

Let
z
be a vertex of
F_{0}
and
z^{*}\in K_{z}
. By
Lemma
9.4
, there exists
a,b\in V(F_{0})
such that
z^{*}a,z^{*}b\in E(G)

and
ab\notin E(G)
. Since there is no
C_{4}
in
G
it should be that
xz^{*}\in E(G)
.
This proves that
x
is complete to
F_{1}
. Hence,
x\in V(F_{2})
, a
contradiction.
∎

Lemma 9.7

Let
a
and
b
be two non-adjacent vertices of some principal hole

C
of
F_{0}
. If some vertex
x
of
V(G)\setminus V(F_{2})
has neighbors
in both
K_{a}
and
K_{b}
, then
a
and
b
have a common neighbor
c

in
C
,
x
is adjacent to
c
, and
x
is anticomplete to every

K_{d}
such that
d\in V(C)\setminus\{a,b,c\}
.

Proof.

Let
a^{*}\in K_{a}
and
b^{*}\in K_{b}
be two neighbors of
x
.
Since
ab\notin E(G)
, by Lemma
9.3
,

\{a^{*},b^{*}\}\cup V(C)\setminus\{a,b\}
induces a hole
C^{*}
. Since

x
is adjacent to
a^{*}
and
b^{*}
, by Lemma
3.10
,
x

has another neighbor
c
in
C^{*}
(and in fact in
C
since
c\neq a^{*},b^{*}
). If
c
is not adjacent to
a^{*}
or
b^{*}
, then
x
is major
w.r.t.
C^{*}
, so by Lemma
3.10
,
C^{*}
and

x
form a universal wheel. It follows that
x
is major w.r.t.
C
, a contradiction to Lemma
9.6
.

We proved that
a
and
b
have a common neighbor
c
in
C
and that

x
is adjacent to
c
. Suppose for a contradiction that
x
has a
neighbor
d^{*}\in K_{d}
where
d\in V(C)\setminus\{a,b,c\}
. By the same
argument as above, since
x
has neighbors in
K_{d}
and
K_{c}
,
c

and
d
must have a common neighbor in
C
, and this common neighbor
must be
a
or
b
, say
a
up to symmetry. So,
x
has neighbors in

K_{d}
and
K_{b}
while
b
and
d
have no common neighbors in
C
, so
we may reach a contradiction as above.
∎

9.2
Connecting vertices of a template

Lemma 9.8

If
x\in A\cup B
and
y\in A^{\prime}\cup B^{\prime}
, then there exists in
G
a
path
P
of length
\ell-2
,
\ell-1
,
\ell
or
\ell+1
from
x
to

y
that contains a principal path.

More specifically:

•

If
x\in A
and
y\in A^{\prime}
, then
P
has length
\ell-2
,
\ell-1
,

\ell
or
\ell+1
.

•

If
x\in A
and
y\in B^{\prime}
, or if
x\in B
and
y\in A^{\prime}
,
then
P
has length
\ell-1
,
\ell
or
\ell+1
.

•

If
x\in B
and
y\in B^{\prime}
, then
P
has length

\ell
or
\ell+1
. Furthermore in that case, there exists another path
Q
from
x
to

y
of length

\ell
or
\ell+1
containing a principal path and
Q
contains no interior vertex of
P
.

Proof.

Suppose first that
x\in A
, say
x=v_{i}
. If
y\in A^{\prime}
, then set

y=v^{\prime}_{j}
. If
i=j
, then
P_{i}
has length
\ell-2
or
\ell-1
. Assume
i\neq j
.
If
v_{i}v_{j}
or
v^{\prime}_{i}v^{\prime}_{j}
, say
v_{i}v_{j}
is an edge then
v_{i}v_{j}P_{v_{j}}v^{\prime}_{j}
is a path of length
\ell-1
or
\ell
.
If none of
v_{i}v_{j}
or
v^{\prime}_{i}v^{\prime}_{j}
is an edge then
v_{i}
and
v_{j}
are in
A_{S}
and
v_{i}P_{v_{i}}v^{\prime}_{i}w^{\prime}v^{\prime}_{j}
is a path of length
\ell
.
If
y\in B^{\prime}
, then one of
v_{i}P_{i}v^{\prime}_{i}y
or
v_{i}P_{i}v^{\prime}_{i}w^{\prime}y

is the path of length at most
\ell+1
we are looking for. The proof is similar when

y\in A^{\prime}
.

We may therefore assume that
x\in B
and
y\in B^{\prime}
. Assume first that there exist
v_{i}
and
v_{j}
in
A
such that
x
is adjacent to
v_{i}
and
v_{j}
and
y
is adjacent to
v^{\prime}_{i}
and
v^{\prime}_{j}
. Then
xv_{i}P_{v_{i}}v^{\prime}_{i}y
and
xv_{j}P_{v_{j}}v^{\prime}_{j}y
are two paths of length
\ell
or
\ell+1
. Consider now the case when there exists one vertex
v_{i}
in
H_{x}
such that
v^{\prime}_{i}
belongs to
H_{y}
. Since both
G[H_{x}]
and
G[H_{y}]
are anticonnected and contain at least two vertices, there exists
u\in H_{x}
non adjacent to
v_{i}
and
v^{\prime}\in H_{y}
non adjacent to
v^{\prime}_{i}
. We may assume that
u^{\prime}
is not adjacent to
y
and that
v
is not adjacent to
x
since else we are in the previous case. So now
v
should be anticomplete to
H_{x}
and
u^{\prime}
should be anticomplete to
H_{y}
. So
\{v_{i},u,v\}
and
\{v^{\prime}_{i},u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\}
are both stable sets of
G
, this is possible if and only if
\{v_{i},u,v\}\subseteq A_{S}
. We have then two paths
xuP_{u}u^{\prime}w^{\prime}y
and
xwvP_{v}v^{\prime}y
of length
\ell+1
. It remains to consider the case where each
v_{i}\in H_{x}
is such that
v^{\prime}_{i}\notin H_{y}
. By definition each of
H_{x}
and
H_{y}
contains a pair of non adjacent vertices and hence there exist two distinct vertices
u,v^{\prime}
such that
u\in H_{x}\cap A_{S}
and
v^{\prime}\in H_{y}\cap A^{\prime}_{S}
. We have then again two paths
xuP_{u}u^{\prime}w^{\prime}y
and
xwvP_{v}v^{\prime}y
of length
\ell+1
.∎

9.3
Connecting vertices of
F_{1}

We here explain how lemmas of Subsection
9.2
are
extended from
F_{0}
to
F_{1}
.

Lemma 9.9

If
u^{*}\in A^{*}\cup B^{*}
and
v^{*}\in A^{\prime*}\cup B^{\prime*}
, then there exists
in
F_{1}
a path
P^{*}
of length
\ell-2
,
\ell-1
,
\ell
or
\ell+1
from
u^{*}

to
v^{*}
that contains the interior of a principal path
P
. More specifically:

•

If
u^{*}\in A^{*}
and
v^{*}\in A^{\prime*}
, then
P^{*}
has length
\ell-2
,
\ell-1
,

\ell
or
\ell+1
.

•

If
u^{*}\in A^{*}
and
v^{*}\in B^{\prime*}
, or if
u^{*}\in B^{*}
and
v^{*}\in A^{\prime*}
,
then
P^{*}
has length
\ell-1
,
\ell
or
\ell+1
.

•

If
u^{*}\in B^{*}
and
v^{*}\in B^{\prime*}
, then
P^{*}
has length

\ell
or
\ell+1
. Furthermore in that case there exists another path
Q^{*}
from
u^{*}

to
v^{*}
of length

\ell
or
\ell+1
which contains the interior of a principal path
Q\neq P
.

Proof.

Let
u
and
v
be such that
u^{*}\in K_{u}
and
v^{*}\in K_{v}
. Let
P

be a path in
F_{0}
like in Lemma
9.8

from
u
to
v
(so
P
contains the interior of some principal path
Q
). By Lemma
9.3
,

\{u^{*},v^{*}\}\cup V(P)\setminus\{u,v\}
induces a path of the same length
as
P
that contains the interior of
Q
.
∎

Lemma 9.10

If in
G
some vertex
x
is adjacent to the ends of a path
P
of length
at most
\ell+1
not containing
x
, then
x
is complete to
V(P)
.

Proof.

Otherwise, a shortest cycle in
G[V(P)\cup\{x\}]
has length at
least 4 and at most
\ell+3
. Since

\ell\geq 4
implies
\ell+3<2\ell
, this is a contradiction.
∎

9.4
Attaching a vertex to
F_{1}

In this subsection, we show that for all vertices
x
of
V(G)\setminus V(F_{2})
,

N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is a clique (see Lemma
9.14
).

Lemma 9.11

If
x\in V(G)\setminus V(F_{2})
has no neighbor in
I^{*}
, then
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is a
clique.

Proof.

Suppose for a contradiction that
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is not a
clique.

(
83)
We may assume that
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)\subseteq A^{*}\cup B^{*}
.

If
x
has neighbors in both
A^{*}\cup B^{*}
and
A^{\prime*}\cup B^{\prime*}
,
then consider a path
P
as in Lemma
9.9
from a
neighbor of
x
in
A^{*}\cup B^{*}
to a neighbor of
x
in

A^{\prime*}\cup B^{\prime*}
. By Lemma
9.10
,
x
is complete to

V(P)
. This is a contradiction since
x
has no neighbor in

I^{*}
. Hence
x
does not have neighbors in both
A^{*}\cup B^{*}
and

A^{\prime*}\cup B^{\prime*}
, and our claim follows up to symmetry.
This proves (83).

(
84)
There exist non-adjacent
a,b\in A
such that
x
has neighbors
in both
K_{a}
and
K_{b}
.

By Lemma
9.5
, since
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is not a clique,
there should exist two non-adjacent vertices
a,b\in V(F_{0})
such
that
x
has a neighbor
a^{*}\in K_{a}
and a neighbor

b^{*}\in K_{b}.
By (
9.4
),
a,b\in A\cup B.

If
a,b\in A
, then our conclusion holds, so we may assume that

b\in B
.

If
a\in A
, then since
ab\notin E(G)
,
H_{b}
is anticomplete to

a
. Let
P^{*}_{a}
be the path induced by

\{a^{*}\}\cup(V(P_{a})\setminus\{a\})
. Let
v\in H_{b}
. We may assume
that
xv\notin E(G)
for otherwise our claim holds (with
a
and

v
). Note that since
ab,av\notin E(G),
by (
c
) of blowup,
a^{*}b^{*},a^{*}v\notin E(G)
.
Now,
a^{*}xb^{*}vP_{v}v^{\prime}a^{\prime}P_{a}^{*}a^{*}
(in case one of
a,v
belongs to
A_{K}
) or
a^{*}xb^{*}vP_{v}v^{\prime}w^{\prime}a^{\prime}P_{a}^{*}a^{*}
(in case both
a,v
belong to
A_{S}
) form a hole of
length
2\ell+1
, a contradiction. Hence, we may assume

a\in B
.

Since
ab\notin E(G)
, by Lemma
8.4
,

\{a\}\cup H_{a}
is
anticomplete to
\{b\}\cup H_{b}
. We may assume that
x
is
anticomplete to
H_{a}\cup H_{b}
for otherwise we may apply the proofs
above. Hence, for
u\in H_{a}
and
v\in H_{b}
,
ua^{*}xb^{*}vP_{v}v^{\prime}u^{\prime}P_{u}u
or
ua^{*}xb^{*}vP_{v}v^{\prime}w^{\prime}u^{\prime}P_{u}u
is
a hole of length
2\ell+2
.
This proves (84).

Now the sets
K_{u}
for all
u\in A\cup A^{\prime}\cup I
,
B^{*}\cup\{x\}

and
B^{\prime*}
form a preblowup of
F_{0}
. All conditions are easily
checked. In particular
x
satisfies condition (
b
)
by (
9.4
) and (
b2
) by (
9.4
)).
So, by Lemma
8.15
,
G[V(F_{1})\cup\{x\}]
is a proper
blowup of some
\ell
-template with
k
principal paths. This contradicts
the maximality of
F_{1}
.
∎

Lemma 9.12

If there exist
x\in V(G)\setminus V(F_{2})
and
u\in A
such that
x

has neighbors in both
K_{u}
and
K_{u^{+}}
and is anticomplete to

K_{u^{++}}
, then
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is a clique.

Proof.

Suppose for a contradiction that
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is not a clique.

(
85)

x
is anticomplete to
A^{\prime*}\cup B^{\prime*}\cup(I^{*}\setminus K_{u^{+}})
.

If
x
has a neighbor
t^{*}
in some
K_{t}
such that

t\in(A^{\prime}\cup I)\setminus\{u^{+}\}
, then note that
t\neq u^{++}
by
assumption. Let
C
be a principal hole that contains
t

and
u
. By Lemma
9.7
applied to
t
and
u
(that are by definition non adjacent in
C
) we should have that
u^{+}
is a common neighbor of
u
and
t
in
C
. This is not possible since the neighbors of
u^{+}
in
C
are
u
and
u^{++}\neq t
. So
x
is anticomplete to
A^{\prime*}\cup(I^{*}\setminus K_{u^{+}})
.

It remains to prove that
x
is anticomplete to
B^{\prime*}
. Otherwise,

x
has a neighbor
t\in B^{\prime*}
. Consider a path
P
from
t
to
a neighbor of
x
in
K_{u}
as in Lemma
9.9
and let
Q
be the principal
path whose interior is contained in
P
. By
Lemma
9.10
,
x
is complete to
V(P)
and hence to
V(Q)
.
This is impossible since we have shown that
x
is anticomplete to
A^{\prime*}
.
This proves (85).

From here on,
u^{*}
and
u^{+*}
are neighbors of
x
in respectively
K_{u}
and
K_{u^{+}}
.

(
86)

x
has a neighbor
y^{*}\in B^{*}
that is
complete to
A^{*}
.

Note that
x
has a neighbor

y^{*}\in K_{y}
for some
y\in A\cup B\setminus\{u\}
, for otherwise,
by (
9.4
),
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)\subseteq K_{u}\cup K_{u^{+}}
and
by Lemma
9.5
,
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is a clique, a
contradiction. In case
y^{*}=w
we are done, so from now on we will assume that
w
is not adjacent to
x
and
y^{*}\neq w
.

Let
v\in A\setminus\{u\}
and assume that there exists
v^{*}\in K_{v}
which not adjacent to
y^{*}
. Then
xv^{*}\notin E(G)
for
otherwise
\{x,y^{*},w,v^{*}\}
induces a
C_{4}
. Let
P^{*}_{v}
be the path induced by

\{v^{*}\}\cup(V(P_{v})\setminus\{v\})
. Either

xy^{*}wv^{*}P^{*}_{v}v^{\prime}w^{\prime}u^{\prime}P_{u}u^{++}u^{+*}x

(in case
v^{\prime}u^{\prime}\notin E(G)
) or

xy^{*}wv^{*}P^{*}_{v}v^{\prime}u^{\prime}P_{u}u^{++}u^{+*}x
(in case
v^{\prime}u^{\prime}\in E(G)
) is a hole of length at least
2\ell+1
. In both cases we get a contradiction. So we have shown that for every
v\in A\setminus\{u\}
,
y^{*}
is complete to
K_{v}
and hence
y
is complete to
A\setminus\{u,y\}
.

We also have that
u^{*}y^{*}\in E(G)
for otherwise,
\{x,y^{*},w,u^{*}\}
induces a
C_{4}
. So, if
y^{*}\in A^{*}
then
A
contains no isolated vertex, a contradiction to Lemma
8.10
. So
y^{*}\in B^{*}
. If
y^{*}

has a non-neighbor
v^{*}\in A^{*}
then from what precedes we have
v^{*}\in K_{u}
. Now there exists
a path
Q
of length 1, 2 or 3 from
x
to
v^{*}
with interior in

K_{u^{+}}
(either
xv^{*}
or
xu^{+*}v^{*}
, or
xu^{+}v^{*}
or
xu^{+*}u^{+}v^{*}
).
Hence,
xQv^{*}wy^{*}x
is a hole of length 4, 5 or 6, a
contradiction. This proves (86).

(
87)

N_{A}(x)\setminus\{u\}=N_{A}(u)
.

If there exists
v\in N_{A}(x)\setminus N_{A}[u]
, then

vP_{v}v^{\prime}u^{\prime}P_{u}u^{++}u^{+*}xv
(in case exactly one of
u,v
is in
A_{K}
) or
vP_{v}v^{\prime}w^{\prime}u^{\prime}P_{u}u^{++}u^{+*}xv
(in case
u,v
are both in
A_{S}
) is a hole of length
2\ell-1
, a
contradiction.

Conversely, suppose there exists
v\in N_{A}(u)\setminus N_{A}(x)
.

Then
v^{\prime}P_{v}vy^{*}xu^{+*}u^{++}P_{u}u^{\prime}w^{\prime}v^{\prime}
(in case exactly one of
u,v
is in
A_{K}
) or
v^{\prime}P_{v}vy^{*}xu^{+*}u^{++}P_{u}u^{\prime}v^{\prime}
(in case
u,v
are both in
A_{K}
) is
a hole of length
2\ell+1
, a contradiction.
This proves (87).

(
88)

x
is complete to
K_{u}
.

Suppose there exists
r\in K_{u}
such that
rx\notin E(G)
.

If
xu^{+}\in E(G)
then
\{r,y^{*},u^{+},x\}
induces a
C_{4}
, a
contradiction. Hence
xu^{+}\notin E(G)
. Now by condition
(
e
) of blowups, either
\{x,y^{*},r,u^{+*}\}
induces a

C_{4}
or
\{x,y^{*},r,u^{+},u^{+*}\}
induces a
C_{5}
.
This proves (88).

Now, the sets
K_{v}
for all
v\in(A\setminus{u})\cup I\cup A^{\prime}
,

K_{u}\cup\{x\}
,
B^{*}
and
B^{\prime*}
form a preblowup of
F_{0}
. All
conditions are easy to check. In particular,
K_{u}\cup\{x\}
is a
clique by (
9.4
), conditions (
a
),
(
b
) and (
i
) follows from (
9.4
),
condition (
a1
) from (
9.4
), condition
(
b1
) from (
9.4
) and condition
(
a2
) from our assumptions.

Hence by Lemma
8.15
,
G[V(F_{1})\cup\{x\}]
is a
proper blowup of some twinless odd
\ell
-template with
k

principal paths that is an induced subgraph of
G
a contradiction
to the maximality of
F_{1}
.
∎

Lemma 9.13

If
x\in V(G)\setminus V(F_{2})
has no neighbor in
B^{*}\cup B^{\prime*}
, then

N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is a clique.

Proof.

Suppose for a contradiction that
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is not a clique. By
Lemma
9.11
,
x
has a neighbor in
I^{*}
. So
x
has a
neighbor in a clique blown up from an internal vertex of some
principal path
P_{v}=v\dots v^{\prime}
. Let
a
(resp.
b
) be the
vertex of
P_{v}
closest to
v
(resp. to
v^{\prime}
) along
P_{v}
and such
that
x
has a neighbor in
K_{a}
(resp.
K_{b}
).

Suppose first that
a=b
, so
a\in I
and
a
is the only vertex of
P_{v}
whose clique contains a neighbor of
x
. Hence, as
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is not a clique,
x
has a neighbour in
some
K_{y}
with
y\in V(F_{0})\setminus V(P_{v})
, and since by assumption

x
has no neighbor in
B^{*}\cup B^{\prime*}
,
y\in A\cup A^{\prime}\cup I
. So,

y
and
a
are non-adjacent members of some principal hole. By
Lemma
9.7
,
x
has a neighbor in some clique
K_{c}

where
c
is adjacent to both
a
and
y
, a contradiction to the properties implied by the supposition that
a=b
.

Suppose now that
ab\in E(G)
. Then
a
and
b
are the only vertices of
P_{v}
whose cliques contain a neighbor of
x
.
If both
a
and
b
are internal
vertices of
P_{v}
, then as in the previous paragraph, we can show that no neighbour of
x
is in
some
K_{y}
with
y\in V(F_{0})\setminus V(P_{v})
and hence
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)\subseteq K_{a}\cup K_{b}
.
So, by Lemma
9.5
,
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is a clique, a
contradiction.
It follows that at least one of
a
or
b
is an
end of
P_{v}
. Up to symmetry, we may assume that
a=v
and

b=v^{+}
. Note that, by the definition of
b
,
x
is then anticomplete to
K_{v^{++}}
. Hence, by
Lemma
9.12
,
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is a clique, a contradiction.

Hence,
a\neq b
and
ab\notin E(G)
. So, by Lemma
9.7
,

a
and
b
have a common neighbor
u
in
P_{v}
. So,
a
,
u
and

b
are consecutive along
P_{v}
(in particular,
u\in I
).

(
89)

x
is complete to
K_{u}
.

Otherwise, let
u^{*}\in K_{u}
be non-adjacent to
x
. There exists
a path
Q_{a}
of length 2 or 3 from
u^{*}
to
x
with interior in

K_{a}
(either
xa^{*}u^{*}
, or
xa^{*}au^{*}
for some
a^{*}
in
K_{a}
).
There exists a similar path
Q_{b}
. So,
Q_{a}
and
Q_{b}
form a
hole of length 4, 5 or 6, a contradiction.
This proves (89).

(
90)

x
is anticomplete to
V(F_{1})\setminus(K_{a}\cup K_{u}\cup K_{b})
.

This follows from Lemma
9.7
and from the assumption that
x
is anticomplete to
B^{*}\cup B^{\prime*}
.
This proves (90).

(
91)

x
has neighbors in each of
K_{a}
,
K_{b}
.

This follows from the definition of
a
and
b
.
This proves (91).

Now the sets
K_{v}
for all
v\in(A\cup A^{\prime}\cup I)\setminus\{u\}
,
K_{u}\cup\{x\}
,

B^{*}
and
B^{\prime*}
form a preblowup of
F_{0}
. All conditions are
easily checked, in particular
K_{u}\cup\{x\}
is a clique
by (
9.4
), it satisfies condition (
i
)
by (
9.4
) and condition (
i1
)
by (
9.4
).

Hence, by Lemma
8.15

G[V(F_{1})\cup\{x\}]
is a
proper blowup of some twinless odd
\ell
-template with
k

principal paths that is an induced subgraph of
G
. This contradicts
the maximality of
F_{1}
.
∎

Lemma 9.14

For all vertices
x
of
V(G)\setminus V(F_{2})
,
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is a clique.

Proof.

Suppose for a contradiction that
N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is not a clique.

(
92)
There exists a principal path
P_{u}=u\dots u^{\prime}
of
F_{0}
such that

x
is anticomplete to
I^{*}\setminus\bigcup_{v\in V(P_{u})}K_{v}
.

Otherwise, there exist two distinct principal paths
P
and
Q
of
F_{0}
such that

a
in the interior of
P
,
b
in the interior of
Q
and

x
has neighbors in both
K_{a}
and
K_{b}
. Note that then
a
and
b
are non adjacent and do not share any neighbour. This contradicts
Lemma
9.7
, applied to the principal hole
C
of
F_{0}
containing
P
and

Q
.
This proves (92).

(
93)
We may assume that
x
has no neighbor in
B^{\prime*}
and has a
neighbor
y^{*}\in K_{y}
where
y\in B
.

Suppose that
x
has a neighbor
u^{*}\in B^{*}
and a neighbor

v^{*}\in B^{\prime*}
. Let
P
and
Q
be as in
Lemma
9.9
. By Lemma
9.10
,
x
is
complete to both
V(P)
and
V(Q)
. In particular,
x
has
neighbors in the interior of two distinct principal paths, a
contradiction to (
9.4
). So, up to symmetry, we
may assume that
x
has no neighbor in
B^{\prime*}
. Hence, by
Lemma
9.13
,
x
has neighbors in
B^{*}
.
This proves (93).

(
94)

x
is adjacent to
u
and
u^{+}
and has a neighbor in

K_{u^{++}}
. Moreover,
x
is anticomplete to

(A^{*}\cup I^{*}\cup A^{\prime*}\cup B^{\prime*})\setminus(K_{u}\cup K_{u^{+}}\cup K_{u^{++}})
.

By Lemma
9.11
,
x
has at least one neighbor in
I^{*}

and by (
9.4
), such a neighbor is in a clique
blown up from an internal vertex of
P_{u}
. So, let
v
be the
vertex of
P_{u}
closest to
u^{\prime}
along
P_{u}
such that
x
has a
neighbor
v^{*}\in K_{v}
. So
v\neq u
and
v\in A^{\prime}\cup I
. We set

Q=y^{*}uP_{u}v
if
y^{*}u\in E(G)
and
Q=y^{*}wuP_{u}v
otherwise. Let

Q^{*}
be the path induced by
\{v^{*}\}\cup(V(Q)\setminus\{v\})
and
observe that
Q^{*}
has length at most
\ell+1
. By
Lemma
9.10
,
x
is complete to
Q^{*}
. If

v\notin\{u^{+},u^{++}\}
, then
x
has neighbors in at least 4
cliques blown up from vertices of
P_{u}
and this contradicts
Lemma
9.7
. If
v=u^{+}
,
x
is adjacent to
u
(since

x
is complete to
Q^{*}
) and anticomplete to
K_{u^{++}}
, so by Lemma
9.12
,

N_{V(F_{1})}(x)
is a clique, a contradiction. So,
v=u^{++}
,
meaning that
x
is adjacent to
u
and
u^{+}
, and is anticomplete
to
I^{*}\setminus(K_{u^{+}}\cup K_{u^{++}})

by (
9.4
).

If
x
has neighbors in some
K_{a}
for
a\in A\setminus\{u\}
then
x

and
C_{u,a}
contradict Lemma
9.7
. Hence
x
is
anticomplete to
A^{*}\setminus\{K_{u}\}
.

By (
9.4
),
x
is anticomplete to
B^{\prime*}
. It remains to
check that
x
is anticomplete to
A^{\prime*}\setminus K_{u^{++}}
. So,
suppose
x
has a neighbor
z^{*}
in some
K_{z}
where

z\in A^{\prime}\setminus\{u^{++}\}
. Then a principal hole that contains
z

and
u
contradicts Lemma
9.7
.
This proves (94).

Let
u^{++*}
be a neighbor of
x
in
K_{u^{++}}
and
P_{u}^{*}
be
the path induced by
(V(P_{u})\setminus\{u^{++}\})\cup\{u^{++*}\}
.

(
95)
For every
z\in B
such that
x
is adjacent to some
z^{*}
in

K_{z}
we have
N_{A}(z)=N_{A}[u]
(in particular
N_{A}(y)=N_{A}[u]
).

Suppose there exists
v\in N_{A}(z)\setminus N_{A}[u]
. By
condition (
d
) or (
f
) of blowups,

vz^{*}\in E(G)
. So, by (
9.4
),

xz^{*}vP_{v}v^{\prime}u^{\prime}P^{*}_{u}u^{++*}x
(if one of
u,v\in A_{K}
) or
xz^{*}vP_{v}v^{\prime}w^{\prime}u^{\prime}P^{*}_{u}u^{++*}x
(if both
u
and
v
are in
A_{S}
) is a hole of length
2\ell-1
, a
contradiction. This proves that

N_{A}(z)\subseteq N_{A}[u]
. In particular,
u
has at
least one neighbor in
H_{z}
, so by condition (
k
) of
templates,
uz\in E(G)
.

Suppose there exists
v\in N_{A}(u)\setminus N_{A}(z)
(so
z\neq w
). By
condition (
c
) of blowups,
vz^{*}\notin E(G)
. By (
9.4
),
xv\notin E(G)
. Hence
xw\in E(G)
, for otherwise
xz^{*}wvP_{v}v^{\prime}u^{\prime}P_{u}^{*}u^{++*}x
or

xz^{*}wvP_{v}v^{\prime}w^{\prime}u^{\prime}P_{u}^{*}u^{++*}x
is a hole of length
2\ell+1
. Since the partition is proper there exists
a vertex
c\in A_{S}
which is isolated in
G[A]
and, since
v\in N(u),

c\neq u
. Again
by (
9.4
),
xc\notin E(G)
and
xwcP_{c}c^{\prime}u^{\prime}P_{u}^{*}u^{++*}x
(in case
u\in A_{K}
) or
xwcP_{c}c^{\prime}w^{\prime}u^{\prime}P_{u}^{*}u^{++*}x
(in case
u\in A_{S}
)
is a hole of length
2\ell-1
, a contradiction.
This proves (95).

(
96)

N_{F_{1}}(x)\subseteq K_{u^{++}}\cup K_{u^{+}}\cup K_{u}\cup K_{y}

By (
9.4
)

N_{F_{1}}(x)\subseteq K_{u^{++}}\cup K_{u^{+}}\cup K_{u}\cup B^{*}
. Suppose there exists
z^{*}\in K_{z}
such that
xz^{*}\in E(G)

and
z\in B\setminus\{y\}
. By (
9.4
),
N_{A}(z)=N_{A}[u]
and

N_{A}(y)=N_{A}[u]
. So, by Lemma
8.9
,
y
and
z
are
twins of
F_{0}
, a contradiction.
This proves (96).

(
97)

y\neq w
.

If
y=w
, then by (
9.4
),

N_{A}(w)=N_{A}[u]=A
and so
u
is a universal vertex of
G[A]
. By Lemma
8.10
, there exists also at least one isolated vertex in
G[A]
, a contradiction to
|A|\geq 3
.
This proves (97).

(
98)

N_{K_{y}}(x)
is complete to
N_{A}[u]
.

By (
9.4
),
N_{A}(y)=N_{A}[u]
. The result follows from
conditions (
d
) and (
f
) of blowups.
This proves (98).

(
99)

x
is complete to
K_{u^{+}}
.

By (
9.4
),
ux\in E(G)
. Suppose for a contradiction
that there exists
u^{+*}\in K_{u^{+}}
non-adjacent to
x
. By
condition (
e
) of blowups,

u^{+*}u,u^{+*}u^{++}\in E(G)
. Hence
xu^{++}\notin E(G)
for
otherwise
\{x,u^{++},u^{+*},u\}
induces a
C_{4}
. But now, either

\{x,u^{++*},u^{+*},u\}
induces a
C_{4}
(if

u^{+*}u^{++*}\in E(G)
) or
\{x,u^{++*},u^{++},u^{+*},u\}

induces a
C_{5}
(if
u^{+*}u^{++*}\notin E(G)
), a contradiction.
This proves (99).

(
100)

K_{u}\cup K_{y}
is a clique.

Since by (
9.4
)
N_{A}(y)=N_{A}[u]
,
u
cannot be an
isolated vertex of
H_{y}
. Hence,
uy
is a solid edge. So, by
condition (
d
) of blowups,
K_{u}
is complete
K_{y}
.
This proves (100).

We define
B_{0}=B^{*}\setminus N_{K_{y}}(x)
.

Now the sets
K_{v}
for all
v\in(A\cup I\cup A^{\prime})\setminus\{u,u^{+}\}
,

K_{u}\cup N_{K_{y}}(x)
,
K_{u^{+}}\cup\{x\}
,
B_{0}
and
B^{\prime*}
form a
preblowup of
F_{0}
. All conditions are easy to check. In particular,

K_{u}\cup N_{K_{y}}(x)
is a clique by (
9.4
),

K_{u^{+}}\cup\{x\}
is a clique by (
9.4
),
conditions (
a
), (
b
) and (
i
) follows from
(
9.4
), condition (
a1
) from
(
9.4
), condition (
a2
) holds because
x

is complete to
N_{K_{y}}(x)
, condition (
i1
) follows from
(
9.4
) and condition (
b1
) holds because of
(
9.4
).

Hence, by Lemma
8.15
,
G[V(F_{1})\cup\{x\}]
is a proper
blowup of some twinless odd
\ell
-template with
k
principal paths
that is an induced subgraph of
G
, a contradiction to the maximality
of
F_{1}
.
∎

9.5
Attaching a component

Lemma 9.15

If
D
is a connected component of
G\setminus F_{2}
, then
N(D)
is a
clique.

Proof.

Suppose that
N(D)
is not a clique. Since
D
is a connected component of
G\setminus F_{2}
we have that
N(D)=N_{V(F_{1})}(D)
. By Lemma
9.2
,

N_{V(F_{1})}(D)
is not a clique. So, there exist
a
and
b
in
D

such that
N_{V(F_{1})}(a)\cup N_{V(F_{1})}(b)
is not a clique, and a
path
P
from
a
to
b
in
D
. We choose
a
and
b
subject to
the minimality of the length of
P
. By
Lemma
9.14
,
a\neq b
(so
P
has length at
least 1).

We set
S^{*}_{a}=N_{V(F_{1})}(a)
and
S^{*}_{b}=N_{V(F_{1})}(b)
. By
Lemma
9.14
,
S^{*}_{a}
and
S^{*}_{b}
are both cliques.
Note that possibly
S^{*}_{a}\cap S^{*}_{b}\neq\varnothing
. We denote by

\text{int}(P)
the set of the internal vertices of
P
. We set

S^{*}_{\circ}=N_{V(F_{1})}(\text{int}(P))
.

We set
S_{a}=\{t\in V(F_{0}):S_{a}^{*}\cap K_{t}\neq\varnothing\}
. We
define
S_{b}
and
S_{\circ}
similarly. Note that
S_{a}
is possibly
not included in
S_{a}^{*}
, and the same remark holds for
S_{b}
and

S_{\circ}
.

(
101)
There exist non-adjacent
x^{*}_{a}\in S^{*}_{a}
and
x^{*}_{b}\in S^{*}_{b}
. Moreover,
for all such
x^{*}_{a}
and
x^{*}_{b}
,
x^{*}_{a}aPbx^{*}_{b}
is a path.

The existence of
x^{*}_{a}
and
x^{*}_{b}
follows from the definition of
a

and
b
, and
x^{*}_{a}aPbx^{*}_{b}
is a path because of the
minimality of
P
.
This proves (101).

(
102)

S^{*}_{a}\cup S^{*}_{\circ}
and
S^{*}_{b}\cup S^{*}_{\circ}
are cliques (in
particular,
S^{*}_{\circ}
is a (possibly empty) clique of
F_{1}

that is complete to both
S^{*}_{a}\setminus S^{*}_{\circ}
and

S^{*}_{b}\setminus S^{*}_{\circ}
).

If
S^{*}_{a}\cup S^{*}_{\circ}
is not a clique, then let
x^{*}y^{*}
be
a non-edge in
S^{*}_{a}\cup S^{*}_{\circ}
. Since
S^{*}_{a}
is a clique
by Lemma
9.14
, we may assume

y^{*}\in S^{*}_{\circ}
. By definition of
S^{*}_{\circ}
,
y^{*}
has a
neighbor in
\text{int}(P)
, and then
x^{*},y^{*}
and some subpath of
P

contradict the minimality of
P
.
The proof is similar for
S^{*}_{b}\cup S^{*}_{\circ}
.
This proves (102).

Note that while
S^{*}_{a}\cup S^{*}_{b}
is not a
clique by assumption, it might be that
S_{a}\cup S_{b}
is a clique
(for instance when
S_{a}=\{u\}
,
S_{b}=\{v\}
and
uv
is an
optional edge of
F_{0}
).

(
103)
If
S^{*}_{\circ}\neq\varnothing
then any two non adjacent vertices
x\in S^{*}_{a}
and
y\in S^{*}_{b}
are at distance
2
in
F_{1}
.

Let
s^{*}\in S^{*}_{\circ}
. By (
9.5
),
s^{*}\neq x,y
and
xs^{*}y
is a path in
F_{1}
.
This proves (103).

(
104)

S_{a}\cup S_{\circ}
and
S_{b}\cup S_{\circ}
are cliques of
F_{0}
(in
particular,
S_{a}
and
S_{b}
are (non-empty) cliques of
F_{0}
and

S_{\circ}
is a (possibly empty) clique of
F_{0}
that is complete
to both
S_{a}\setminus S_{\circ}
and
S_{b}\setminus S_{\circ}
).

If
S_{a}\cup S_{\circ}
is not a clique, then let
xy
be a
non-edge of
S_{a}\cup S_{\circ}
. Since

x\in S_{a}\cup S_{\circ}
, there exists

x^{*}\in K_{x}\cap(S^{*}_{a}\cup S^{*}_{\circ})
and

y^{*}\in K_{y}\cap(S^{*}_{a}\cup S^{*}_{\circ})
. By
condition (
c
) of blowups, since
xy\notin E(G)
,

K_{x}
is anticomplete to
K_{y}
. So,
x^{*}y^{*}\notin E(G)
, a
contradiction to (
9.5
).

The proof is similar for
S_{b}\cup S_{\circ}
.
This proves (104).

For the next claim we use the path
P
defined at the very beginning of the proof.

(
105)
If a hole
C
of
F_{1}
contains two non adjacent vertices

x\in S^{*}_{a}
and
y\in S^{*}_{b}
, then
P
and
C
form either:

•

a theta, and
S^{*}_{a}\cap V(C)=\{x\}
,
S^{*}_{b}\cap V(C)=\{y\}
and the
three paths, all of length
\ell
, are the two paths between
x
and
y
in

C
and the path
between
x
and
y
obtained by adding the edges
ax
and
by
to
P
; or

•

a prism,
S^{*}_{a}\cap V(C)=\{x,z\}
,
S^{*}_{b}\cap V(C)=\{y,t\}
, the triangles of the prism are
axz
and
byt
and the
three disjoint paths all of length
\ell-1
, are either:

–

the path
P
, the shortest path between
x
and
y
in
C
and the shortest path
between
z
and
t
in
C
; or

–

the path
P
, the shortest path between
x
and
t
in
C
and the shortest path between
z
and
y
in
C
.

Note that since
S^{*}_{a}
is a clique,

S^{*}_{a}\cap V(C)
contains
x
and at most one other vertex which
should be adjacent to
x
. The same holds for
S^{*}_{b}
and
y
.

Let us assume that
S^{*}_{\circ}\cap V(C)\neq\varnothing
. Then
by (
9.5
), there exists a unique
vertex
t\in S^{*}_{\circ}\cap V(C)
, and
t
is such that

S^{*}_{a}\cap V(C)\subseteq\{x,t\}
and

S^{*}_{b}\cap V(C)\subseteq\{y,t\}
. Hence
C
and
P
form a
proper wheel centered at
t
, a contradiction to
Lemma
3.10
. So,

S^{*}_{\circ}\cap V(C)=\varnothing
.

If
a
and
b
have a common neighbor
t
in
C
, then
x
and
y

are the two neighbors of
t
in
C
and so,
C
and
P
form a
proper wheel centered at
t
, again a contradiction to
Lemma
3.10
. So the neighborhoods of
a
and
b

in
C
are disjoint.

From the remarks above and Lemma
3.10
, we obtain that
C
and
P
form a theta whose three paths have length
\ell
or a prism whose three paths have length
\ell-1
. This can happen only
if we are in one of the three cases described in (
9.5
).

This proves (105).

(
106)

S_{a}\cap I=S_{b}\cap I=\varnothing
.

Otherwise, up to symmetry,
S_{a}\cap I\neq\varnothing
. So, there
exists a principal path
P_{u}=u\dots u^{\prime}
of
F_{0}
whose interior
intersects
S_{a}
. By (
9.5
),
S_{a}
is a clique, so

1\leq|S_{a}|\leq 2
and
S_{a}\subseteq V(P_{u})
.
We now break into three cases.

Case 1:

S_{b}\subseteq V(P_{u})
.

By (
9.5
) there exist vertices
x_{a}
and

x_{b}
of
P_{u}
such that there exist non adjacent vertices
x^{*}_{a}\in S^{*}_{a}\cap K_{x_{a}}
and
x^{*}_{b}\in S^{*}_{b}\cap K_{x_{b}}
.

We first show that there exist such
x_{a}
and
x_{b}
that are not adjacent. Otherwise, and since
S_{a}\subseteq V(P_{u})
and
S_{b}\subseteq V(P_{u})
, we have that
S_{a}\cup S_{b}=\{x_{a},x_{b}\}
. By replacing
x_{a}
and
x_{b}
by
x^{*}_{a}
and
x^{*}_{b}
in any principal hole
C
containing
P_{u}
we obtain a path
P_{C}
of length
2\ell-1
, by (
9.5
)
S^{*}_{\circ}\cap P_{C}=\varnothing
and so
V(P_{C})\cup V(P)
induces a hole of length at least
2\ell+2
, a contradiction. So we may assume that
x_{a}
and
x_{b}
are not adjacent.

Let
C
be any principal hole of
F_{0}
that contains

P_{u}
. By Lemma
9.3
,

\{x^{*}_{a},x^{*}_{b}\}\cup(V(C)\setminus\{x_{a},x_{b}\})
induces a hole

C^{*}
. Let us apply (
9.5
) to
C^{*}
,
x^{*}_{a}
and

x^{*}_{b}
. We obtain that the shortest path in
C^{*}
between
x^{*}_{a}

and
x^{*}_{b}
has length
\ell
or
\ell-1
.
However the path
P^{*}
obtained from
P_{u}
by replacing
x_{a}
by
x^{*}_{a}
and
x_{b}
by

x^{*}_{b}
is contained in
C^{*}
and it has length at most
\ell-1
,
So wlog,
x_{a}=u
and
x_{b}=u^{\prime}
.
We should then be in the second situation described in (
9.5
) and there should exist
z\in S^{*}_{a}\cap V(C)
, and
t\in S^{*}_{b}\cap V(C)
such that the shortest path between them on
C^{*}
has length
\ell-1
and is disjoint from
P^{*}
, a contradiction to the assumption that
S_{a}
and
S_{b}\subseteq V(P_{u})
.

Case 2:

S_{b}
contains a vertex of some principal
path
P_{v}
distinct from
P_{u}
. Up to symmetry, since
S_{b}
is a
clique (by (
9.5
)), we assume that
b
is anticomplete to
K_{v^{\prime}}
.

Let
y
be the vertex of
P_{u}
closest to
u^{\prime}
such that
a
has a
neighbor
y^{*}\in K_{y}
. Let
z
be the vertex of
P_{v}
closest to

v
such that
b
has a neighbor
z^{*}\in K_{z}
. Possibly
y=u^{\prime}

and
z=v
, but
z\neq v^{\prime}
and
y\neq u
since
a
has a neighbor in

I^{*}
by assumption. In particular,
yz\notin E(G)
. By
condition (
c
) of blowups,
y^{*}z^{*}\notin E(G)
.

Let
C
be the principal hole of
F_{0}
that contains
P_{u}
and

P_{v}
. By Lemma
9.3
,

\{y^{*},z^{*}\}\cup(V(C)\setminus\{y,z\})
induces a hole
C^{*}
.
Applying (
9.5
) to
C^{*}
,
y^{*}
and
z^{*}
, we obtain that

P
has length
\ell-2
or
\ell-1
. We denote by
P^{*}_{u}
the path
obtained from
P_{u}
by replacing
y
by
y^{*}
, and by
P^{*}_{v}
the
path obtained from
P_{v}
by replacing
z
by
z^{*}
. Let
P^{*}
be
the path
vP^{*}_{v}z^{*}bPay^{*}P^{*}_{u}u^{\prime}
(in case
z=v
one should replace
vP^{*}_{v}z^{*}
by
z^{*}
, and in case
y=u^{\prime}
one should replace
y^{*}P^{*}_{u}u^{\prime}
by
y^{*}
). The length of
P^{*}
is at least

\ell
.

Consider now a vertex
r\in A\setminus\{u,v\}.
Depending on the adjacencies of
r
with
v
and of
r^{\prime}
with
u^{\prime}
, one of

rvP^{*}u^{\prime}w^{\prime}r^{\prime}P_{r}r
or
rwvP^{*}u^{\prime}w^{\prime}r^{\prime}P_{r}r
or
rwvP^{*}u^{\prime}r^{\prime}P_{r}r
or
rvP^{*}u^{\prime}r^{\prime}P_{r}r
(with possibly
v
replaced by
z^{*}
in case
z=v
and
u^{\prime}
replaced by
y^{*}
in case
y=u
) is a cycle
C_{r}
with at most one possible chord
br
or
bw
in case
z=v
. Hence the length of this cycle should be at most
2\ell+1
. Since the length of
P_{r}
is at least
\ell-2
the case where
C_{r}=rwvP^{*}u^{\prime}w^{\prime}r^{\prime}P_{r}r
cannot occur and we get that at least one of
vr
,
u^{\prime}r^{\prime}
is an edge of
G
and so at least one of
u,v,r
is in
A_{K}
.
We also notice that if the length of
P^{*}
is at least
\ell+1
then
vr
and
u^{\prime}r^{\prime}
should be edges of
G
,
r\in A_{S},

u,v\in A_{K}
,
z=v
,
y=u^{\prime}
and
br
is a chord of the cycle
rz^{*}P^{*}y^{*}r^{\prime}P_{r}r
.
This should be valid for any
r\in A\setminus\{u,v\}
. However since the partition is proper,
A_{S}
contains at least
2
elements and we get a contradiction to the fact that
S_{b}
is a clique.
Hence
P^{*}
has length
\ell
,
y=u^{\prime}
,
z=v
and
P
has length
\ell-2
.

So by (
9.5
),
P
and
C^{*}
form a theta.
However, since we have assumed
S_{a}\cap I\neq\varnothing
,
a
has a neighbor
t^{*}\in K_{u^{-}}
where
u^{-}
is the neighbor of
u^{\prime}
in
P_{u}
. By Lemma
9.3
, since
S^{*}_{a}
is a clique, we have that

\{t^{*}\}\cup(V(C^{*})\setminus\{u^{-}\})
induces a hole. This hole and
P
form a pyramid, a contradiction to (
9.5
).

Case 3:
We are neither in Case 1 nor in Case 2.

Since we are not in Case 1,
S_{b}
contains a vertex of
F_{0}\setminus P_{u}
,
and since we are not in Case 2, this vertex must be in
B\cup B^{\prime}
.
Up to symmetry, we assume that
S_{b}\cap B\neq\varnothing
. Since

S_{b}
is a clique (by (
9.5
)),
S_{b}\cap(B^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime}\cup I)=\varnothing
.
Hence,
S_{b}\subseteq B\cup\{u\}
and there exist
x\in B\cap S_{b}
and
x^{*}\in K_{x}\cap S^{*}_{b}
.

Let
u_{a}
be the vertex of
S_{a}
which is the closest to
u
in

P_{u}
and let
u^{\prime}_{a}
be the vertex of
S_{a}
which is the closest to

u^{\prime}
in
P_{u}
. Notice that, since
S_{a}
is a clique (by (
6.4
)), either
u_{a}=u^{\prime}_{a}
or
u_{a}u^{\prime}_{a}
is an edge. So it may be that
u_{a}=u
or
u^{\prime}_{a}=u^{\prime}
but since
S_{a}\cap I\neq\varnothing
we know that
u_{a}\neq u^{\prime}
and
u^{\prime}_{a}\neq u
. Let now

u^{*}_{a}\in K_{u_{a}}\cap S^{*}_{a}
and
u^{\prime*}_{a}\in K_{u^{\prime}_{a}}\cap S^{*}_{a}
. We denote by
P^{*}_{u}
the path obtained from
P_{u}
by replacing
u_{a}
by
u^{*}_{a}

and, in case
u_{a}\neq u^{\prime}_{a}
, by replacing
u^{\prime}_{a}
by
u^{\prime*}_{a}
.
Notice that if
u^{*}_{a}\neq u^{\prime*}_{a}
then

u^{*}_{a}u^{\prime*}_{a}\in E(G)
since
S_{a}^{*}
is a clique.

Suppose that
u^{\prime}_{a}=u^{+}
, where
u^{+}
is the neighbor of
u
in
P_{u}
. Since
H_{x}
contains at least two vertices there exists
v\in H_{x}\setminus\{u\}
. By (
6.4
), by the fact that
P
contains at least one edge, and because
P_{u}
and
P_{v}
belong to a hole of
F_{0}

of length
2\ell
in which
u^{+}
and
v
are at distance at most
3
, one of
aPbx^{*}vP_{v}v^{\prime}u^{\prime}P^{*}_{u}u^{\prime*}_{a}a
or
aPbx^{*}vP_{v}v^{\prime}w^{\prime}u^{\prime}P^{*}_{u}u^{\prime*}_{a}a
is a hole of length at least
2\ell+1
, a contradiction. Hence from now on, we may assume that
u_{a}\neq u
(hence
a
is not adjacent to
u
) and that if
u_{a}=u^{+}
then
u^{\prime}_{a}\neq u_{a}
. Now by (
6.4
) we get that
S_{\circ}=\varnothing
.

Suppose first that
u\in H_{x}
. Let
v\in H_{x}
be non-adjacent to

u
(this is possible since
G[H_{x}]
is anticonnected and contains more than one vertex). So,
P_{u}
,

P_{v}
,
x
and possibly
w^{\prime}
form a hole
C
(possibly not principal). Let
z
be the
vertex in
I\cap S_{a}
which is the closest to
u
in
P_{u}
and let

z^{*}\in K_{z}\cap S^{*}_{a}
. By
Lemma
9.3
,

\{x^{*},z^{*}\}\cup V(C)\setminus\{x,z\}
induces a hole
C^{*}
of
F_{1}

(
ux^{*},vx^{*}\in E(G)
by condition (
f
) of blowups). The
distance between
x^{*}
and
z^{*}
in
C^{*}
is at most
\ell-1
since

P_{u}
has length at most
\ell-1
, a contradiction to (
9.5
)
applied to
C^{*}
,
x^{*}
and
z^{*}
(we cannot have a theta because
x^{*}
and
z^{*}
are too close in
C^{*}
and we cannot have a prism because else
b
would have a neighbor in
K_{v}
, a contradiction to
S_{b}\subseteq B\cup\{u\}
). Hence, from here on, we may assume that no vertex

x\in B\cap S_{b}
is such that
u\in H_{x}
.

Suppose now that

u\in N(H_{x})
. Since
S_{b}\subseteq B\cup\{u\}
,
we have
S_{b}\cap H_{x}=\varnothing
. Depending on whether
b
is
adjacent to
u
or not, one of
u^{*}_{a}aPbuP^{*}_{u}u^{*}_{a}
or

u^{*}_{a}aPbx^{*}uP^{*}_{u}u^{*}_{a}
(remind that
u_{a}\neq u
) is a hole, implying that
P
has length at
least
\ell-1
. Let
v\in H_{x}
, then
uv
is an edge (since
u\in N(H_{x})
). So,
x^{*}bPau^{\prime*}_{a}P^{*}_{u}u^{\prime}w^{\prime}v^{\prime}P_{v}vx^{*}
or
x^{*}bPau^{\prime*}_{a}P^{*}_{u}u^{\prime}v^{\prime}P_{v}vx^{*}

(in case
u^{\prime*}_{a}=u^{\prime}
one should replace
u^{\prime*}_{a}P^{*}_{u}u^{\prime}
by
u^{\prime*}_{a}
)
is a hole of length at least
2\ell+1
, a contradiction.

Hence, from here on, we may assume that no vertex in
B\cap S_{b}
is adjacent to
u
and so
w\notin B\cap S_{b}
, in particular
x\neq w
and
x^{*}
is not adjacent to
u
. Then to avoid a
C_{4}
induced by
\{b,x^{*},w,u\}
,
b
is not adjacent to
u
and
u^{*}_{a}aPbx^{*}wuP^{*}_{u}u^{*}_{a}
is a hole, implying that
P
has length at least
\ell-2
.
So, for any
v\in H_{x}
, the hole

x^{*}bPau^{\prime*}_{a}P^{*}_{u}u^{\prime}v^{\prime}P_{v}vx^{*}
or the hole
x^{*}bPau^{\prime*}_{a}P^{*}_{u}u^{\prime}w^{\prime}v^{\prime}P_{v}vx^{*}
(in case
u^{\prime}_{a}=u^{\prime}
one should
replace
u^{\prime*}_{a}P^{*}u^{\prime}
by
u^{\prime*}_{a}
) has length
2\ell
if and only if
P
has length
\ell-2
,
u^{\prime}_{a}=u^{\prime}
,
u^{\prime}
is adjacent to
v^{\prime}
and
P_{v}
has length
\ell-2
. This implies that
v\in A_{S}
and
u\in A_{K}
. Furthermore, since
S_{a}\cap I\neq\varnothing
and
S_{a}
is a clique we get that
u_{a}
is the neighbor of
u^{\prime}
on the path
P_{u}
.

Assume there exists
r\in A_{S}
which is adjacent to
u
. Then
r\notin H_{x}
and
rP_{r}r^{\prime}w^{\prime}u^{\prime*}_{a}aPbx^{*}wr
is a hole of length
2\ell+2
, a contradiction. Hence no such
r
exists and by setting
\mathbb{A}_{K}=(A_{K}\setminus\{u\})\cup\{x^{*}\},\mathbb{A}_{S}=A_{S}\cup\{u\},\mathbb{B}=\{w\},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{K}=(A^{\prime}_{K}\setminus\{u^{\prime}\})\cup\{a\},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{S}=A^{\prime}_{S}\cup\{u^{*}_{a}\},\mathbb{B}^{\prime}=\{u^{\prime*}_{a}\}

we obtain an even pretemplate partition contained in
G
with
k+1
paths (the new paths are
uP_{u}u^{*}_{a}
of length
\ell-2
and
x^{*}bPa
of length
\ell-1
), a contradiction.

This proves (106).

(
107)
We may assume that
S_{a}\subseteq A\cup B
and

S_{b}\subseteq A^{\prime}\cup B^{\prime}
.

Otherwise, by (
9.5
) and since
S_{a}
and
S_{b}
are cliques (by (
9.5
)),
we may assume that
S_{a},S_{b}\subseteq A\cup B
.

We will show that there exists a path
Q^{*}
of length at
least
2\ell-2
whose union with
P
induces a hole. This is a
contradiction because it implies that
P
has length at most
0
.
So, to conclude the proof, it remains to prove the existence of

Q^{*}
.

By (
9.5
), there exist non-adjacent
x^{*}_{a}\in S^{*}_{a}
and
x^{*}_{b}\in S^{*}_{b}
and
for all such
x^{*}_{a}
and
x^{*}_{b}
,
x^{*}_{a}aPbx^{*}_{b}
is a path.
Let
x_{a}
and
x_{b}
be the vertices of
F_{0}
such
that
x^{*}_{a}\in K_{x_{a}}
and
x^{*}_{b}\in K_{x_{b}}
. Note that
possibly
x_{a}x_{b}
is an edge, but this happens only if
x_{a}x_{b}
is
an optional edge of
F_{0}
(since
x^{*}_{a}x^{*}_{b}
is not an edge). We break into three cases.

Case 1:

x_{a},x_{b}\in A.

Then
x_{a}x_{b}\notin E(G)
(otherwise
x^{*}_{a}
and
x^{*}_{b}
would be adjacent) and at least one of
x_{a},x_{b}
belongs to
A_{S}
. So from the definition of templates, there exists a path
Q

of length
2\ell-2
from
x_{a}
to
x_{b}
consisting in
P_{x_{a}}
and
P_{x_{b}}
joined either by an edge
x^{\prime}_{a}x^{\prime}_{b}
or by a path
x^{\prime}_{a}w^{\prime}x^{\prime}_{b}
. By Lemma
9.3
,

\{x^{*}_{a},x^{*}_{b}\}\cup(V(Q)\setminus\{x_{a},x_{b}\})
induces the path

Q^{*}
that we are looking for. Note that
Q^{*}
and
P
form a hole
by (
9.5
) and our assumption that

S_{a},S_{b}\subseteq A\cup B
.

Case 2:

x_{a}\in A
and
x_{b}\in B.

Whether
x_{a}x_{b}
is an optional edge or a non-edge, an immediate
consequence of the definition of a template is that there exists a
vertex
z\in H_{x_{b}}
that is non-adjacent to
x_{a}
. By (
9.5
),
S_{a}
is a clique so
z\notin S_{a}
. We may
furthermore assume that
z\notin S_{b}
since else we are in the same
situation as in Case 1. By definition of a template, there exists a
path
Q_{0}
of length
2\ell-2
between
x_{a}
and
z
consisting in
P_{x_{a}}
and
P_{z}
joined either by an edge
x^{\prime}_{a}z^{\prime}
or by a path
x^{\prime}_{a}w^{\prime}z^{\prime}
. Then by
Lemma
9.3
,
\{x^{*}_{a},x^{*}_{b}\}\cup(V(x_{b}zQ_{0}x_{a})\setminus\{x_{a},x_{b}\})
induces a path
Q^{*}
of length

2\ell-1
. Note that
Q^{*}
and
P
form a hole
by (
9.5
) and our assumption that

S_{a},S_{b}\subseteq A\cup B
,
z\notin S_{b}
and
z\notin S_{a}
.

Case 3:

x_{a},x_{b}\in B.

Then
x_{a}x_{b}\notin E(G)
(otherwise
x^{*}_{a}
and
x^{*}_{b}
would be adjacent). Hence, by Lemma
8.4
,

H_{x_{a}}\cup\{x_{a}\}
is anticomplete to
H_{x_{b}}\cup\{x_{b}\}
. So,
let
u_{a}\in H_{x_{a}}
and
u_{b}\in H_{x_{b}}
, we may assume that
u_{a},u_{b}\notin S_{a}\cup S_{b}
since else we
are in the situation of Case 1 or 2. By definition of a template, there exists a
path
Q_{0}
of length
2\ell-2
between
u_{a}
and
u_{b}
consisting in
P_{u_{a}}
and
P_{u_{b}}
joined either by an edge
u^{\prime}_{a}u^{\prime}_{b}
or by a path
u^{\prime}_{a}w^{\prime}u^{\prime}_{b}
. By Lemma
9.3
,

Q^{*}=x^{*}_{a}u_{a}Q_{0}u_{b}x^{*}_{b}
is also a path, it is of length
2\ell
.
Now,
it is easy to verify that
Q^{*}
and
P
form a hole of length more than
2\ell
, a contradiction..

This proves (107).

(
108)

S_{\circ}=\varnothing
.

By (
9.5
) and (
9.5
), if

S_{\circ}\neq\varnothing
, then
\ell=4
, and there exists a principal
path
P_{u}=ucu^{\prime}
of
F_{0}
such that
S_{a}=\{u\},

S_{b}=\{u^{\prime}\}

and
S_{\circ}=\{c\}
. Let
u^{*}\in K_{u}\cap S^{*}_{a}
,
c^{*}\in S^{*}_{\circ}
and

u^{\prime*}\in K_{u^{\prime}}\cap S^{*}_{b}
. Observe that by (
9.5
), each of
u^{*}c^{*},u^{\prime*}c^{*}
is an edge and by definition,
c^{*}
has a neighbor in
\text{int}(P)
.

Let
P_{v}=v\dots v^{\prime}
be any principal path distinct from
P_{u}
. Now,
P_{v}
,
P
,
u^{*}
,

u^{\prime*}
,
c^{*}
and possibly
w
and/or
w^{\prime}
form a proper wheel centered at
c^{*}
, a
contradiction to Lemma
3.10
.
This proves (108).

(
109)

(S_{a}\cup S_{b})\cap(A_{S}\cup A^{\prime}_{S})=\varnothing
.

Notice that each of
S_{a}\cap A_{S}
and
S_{b}\cap A^{\prime}_{S}
contains at most one vertex since
S_{a}
and
S_{b}
are cliques.

Case 1:

S_{a}
contains
x\in A_{S}
and
S_{b}
contains
y^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}_{S}
.

Let
x^{*}\in K_{x}\cap S^{*}_{a}
and
y^{\prime*}\in K_{y^{\prime}}\cap S^{*}_{b}
. We denote by
P^{*}_{x}
(resp.
P^{*}_{y}
) the path obtained from
P_{x}
(resp.
P_{y}
) by replacing
x
(resp.
y^{\prime}
) by
x^{*}
(resp.
y^{\prime*}
).
If
x=y
then
ax^{*}P^{*}_{x}x^{\prime}bPa
is a hole, so
P
has length
\ell
. Since the partition is proper there exists an other vertex
r\in A_{S}
and
ax^{*}wrP_{r}r^{\prime}w^{\prime}y^{\prime*}bPa
is a cycle of length
2\ell+4
whose possible chords are
aw
and
bw^{\prime}
, in any case the cycle contains a too long hole. Hence
x\neq y
.

Assume that
aw,bw^{\prime}\notin E(G)
.
Then
ax^{*}P^{*}_{x}x^{\prime}w^{\prime}y^{\prime*}bPa
is a hole and hence
P
has length
\ell-2
. We know that the template has at least one more principal path
P_{r}
. In case there exists
r\in A_{S}
then
ax^{*}wrP_{r}r^{\prime}w^{\prime}y^{\prime*}bPa
is a hole of length
2\ell+2
, a contradiction. So any
r\in A\setminus\{x,y\}
belongs to
A_{K}
. If
rx
and
ry
are in
E(G)
then
ax^{*}rP_{r}r^{\prime}w^{\prime}y^{\prime*}bPa
is a cycle of length
2\ell+2
whose only possible chord is
ar
, so we get a contradiction. So by symmetry we get that
r
is adjacent to exactly one of
x
and
y
and
r^{\prime}
is adjacent to exactly one of
x^{\prime}
and
y^{\prime}
. In case
r
is adjacent to
y
and not to
x
then
ax^{*}wrP_{r}r^{\prime}w^{\prime}y^{\prime*}bPa
is a hole of length
2\ell+3
, a contradiction. So we may assume that
r
is adjacent to
x
and not to
y
(and then
r^{\prime}
is adjacent to
y^{\prime}
and not to
x^{\prime}
), and this is true for any
r\in A_{K}
.

Let
x^{+}
be the neighbour of
x
in
P_{x}
and
y^{-}
be the neighbour of
y^{\prime}
in
P_{y}
. By setting
\mathbb{A}_{K}=A_{K},\mathbb{A}_{S}=\{a,x^{+},w\},\mathbb{B}=\{x^{*}\},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{K}=A^{\prime}_{K},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{S}=\{b,w^{\prime},y^{-}\},\mathbb{B}^{\prime}=\{y^{\prime*}\}

we obtain an even pretemplate partition contained in
G
with
k+1
paths (the new paths are
aPb
,
x^{+}P_{x}x^{\prime}w^{\prime}
,
wyP_{y}y^{-}
each of length
\ell-1
), a contradiction. Hence
aw
or
bw^{\prime}
is an edge of
G
.

So by symmetry we may set that
aw\in E(G)
.
Then
awyP_{y}^{*}y^{\prime*}bPa
is a hole, so
P
has length
\ell-1
. If
bw^{\prime}\notin E(G)
then
ax^{*}P^{*}_{x}x^{\prime}w^{\prime}y^{\prime*}bPa
is a hole of length
2\ell+1
, a contradiction. So
bw^{\prime}\in E(G)
.
Suppose
A_{S}
contains a vertex
r\neq x,y
then
awrP_{r}r^{\prime}w^{\prime}y^{\prime*}bPa
is hole of length
2\ell+1
, a contradiction. Hence
A_{S}=\{x,y\}
and
ar,br^{\prime}\in E(G)
for every
r\in A_{K}
.

So, by setting
\mathbb{A}_{K}=A_{K}\cup\{a\},\mathbb{A}_{S}=\{x^{*},y\},\mathbb{B}=\{w\},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{K}=A^{\prime}_{K}\cup\{b\},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{S}=\{x^{\prime},y^{\prime*}\},\mathbb{B}^{\prime}=\{w^{\prime}\}

we obtain an even pretemplate partition contained in
G
with
k+1
paths (the new paths are
aPb
of length
\ell-1
and
P^{*}_{x},P^{*}_{y}
of length
\ell-2
), a contradiction.

Case 2:

S_{a}\cap A_{S}=\varnothing
,
S_{a}
contains a vertex
x\in A_{K}
and
S_{b}
contains a vertex
y^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}_{S}
(symmetric to the case where
S_{b}\cap A^{\prime}_{S}=\varnothing
,
S_{b}
contains a vertex
y^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}_{K}
and
S_{a}
contains a vertex
x\in A_{S}
).

Let
x^{*}\in K_{x}\cap S^{*}_{a}
and
y^{\prime*}\in K_{y^{\prime}}\cap S^{*}_{b}
.
Suppose that
xy\in E(G)
. Then
ax^{*}yP^{*}_{y}y^{\prime*}bPa
is a hole and hence
P
has length
\ell-1
. Then
ax^{*}P^{*}_{x}x^{\prime}w^{\prime}y^{\prime*}bPa
is a cycle with at most one possible chord (
bw^{\prime}
) of length
2\ell+2
, a contradiction. So
xy\notin E(G)
and consequently
x^{\prime}y^{\prime}\in E(G)
. Since
x^{*}
and
y^{\prime*}
both belong to the hole
x^{*}P_{x}x^{\prime}y^{\prime*}P^{*}_{y}ywx^{*}
, by (
9.5
) we get that
P
and this hole induce either a theta or a prism.

- Subcase 2.1:
x^{*}P^{*}_{x}x^{\prime}y^{\prime*}P^{*}_{y}ywx^{*}
and
P
induce a theta.

Then
P
has length
\ell-2
and
aw,bx^{\prime}\notin E(G)
.
Assume that
y
has a neighbour
v
in
A
. Then since
y\in A_{S}
we have that
v\in A_{K}
and hence
v^{\prime}y^{\prime*}\notin E(G)
and
xv,x^{\prime}v^{\prime}\in E(G)
. Hence to avoid a
C_{4}
induced by
\{b,x^{\prime},y^{\prime*},v^{\prime}\}
it should be that
bv^{\prime}\notin E(G)
. So
aPby^{\prime*}x^{\prime}v^{\prime}P_{v}vxa
is a cycle of length
2\ell+2
whose only possible chord is
va
, a contradiction to the fact that
G
belongs to
C_{2\ell}
.
We may then conclude that
y
has no neighbor in
A
and so by setting
\mathbb{A}_{K}=(A_{K}\setminus\{x\})\cup\{x^{*},w\},\mathbb{A}_{S}=(A_{S}\setminus\{y\})\cup\{a\},\mathbb{B}=\varnothing,\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{K}=A^{\prime}_{K}\cup\{y^{\prime*}\},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{S}=(A^{\prime}_{S}\setminus\{y^{\prime}\})\cup\{b\},\mathbb{B}^{\prime}=B^{\prime}
,
we obtain an even pretemplate partition contained in
G
with
k+1
paths (the new paths are
aPb
of length
\ell-2
and
x^{*}P^{*}_{x}x^{\prime}
and
wyP^{*}_{y}y^{\prime*}
of length
\ell-1
,
\mathbb{A}_{S}
and
\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{S}
are both stable sets), a contradiction.

- Subcase 2.2:
x^{*}P^{*}_{x}x^{\prime}y^{\prime*}P^{*}_{y}ywx^{*}
and
P
induce a prism. Then
P
has length
\ell-1
and
aw,bx^{\prime}\in E(G)
.

So, by setting
\mathbb{A}_{K}=(A_{K}\setminus\{x\})\cup\{x^{*},a\},\mathbb{A}_{S}=A_{S},\mathbb{B}=B,\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{K}=A^{\prime}_{K}\cup\{b\},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{S}=(A^{\prime}_{S}\setminus\{y^{\prime}\})\cup\{y^{\prime*}\},\mathbb{B}^{\prime}=B^{\prime}

we obtain an even pretemplate partition contained in
G
with
k+1
paths (the new paths are
aPb
,
x^{*}P^{*}_{x}x^{\prime}
and
yP^{*}_{y}y^{\prime*}
of lengths respectively
\ell-1
,
\ell-1
,
\ell-2
), a contradiction.

Case 3:

S_{a}\cap A=\varnothing
,
S_{a}
contains a vertex
x\in B
and
S_{b}
contains a vertex
y^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}_{S}
(symmetric to the case where
S_{b}\cap A^{\prime}=\varnothing
,
S_{b}
contains a vertex
y^{\prime}\in B^{\prime}
and
S_{a}
contains a vertex
x\in A_{S}
).

If
xy\in E(G)
then because of the hole
ax^{*}yP_{y}y^{\prime*}bPa
the path
P
has length
\ell-1
and once again it is enough to replace
x
by
x^{*}
,
y^{\prime}
by
y^{\prime*}
and add
a
to
A_{K}
and
b
to
A^{\prime}_{K}
in order to obtain an
\ell
-pretemplate partition with
k+1
principal paths, hence
xy\notin E(G)
. So, we may assume that
a
is not adjacent to
w
. Then because of
ax^{*}wyP^{*}_{y}y^{\prime*}bPa
, the path
P
has length
\ell-2
.

So, by setting
\mathbb{A}_{K}=A_{K}\cup\{x^{*}\},\mathbb{A}_{S}=A_{S},\mathbb{B}=\{w\},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{K}=A^{\prime}_{K}\cup\{b\},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{S}=(A^{\prime}_{S}\setminus\{y^{\prime}\})\cup\{y^{\prime*}\},\mathbb{B}^{\prime}=B^{\prime}

we obtain an even pretemplate partition contained in
G
with
k+1
paths (the new paths are
x^{*}aPb
of length
\ell-1
and
yP^{*}_{y}y^{\prime*}
of length
\ell-2
), a contradiction.

This proves (109).

We may now conclude the proof. By (
9.5
) we have that
S_{a}\subseteq A_{K}\cup B
and
S_{b}\subseteq A^{\prime}_{K}\cup B^{\prime}
. Notice that if the length of
P
is
\ell-1
(resp.
\ell-2
) then we get a contradiction since we can add
a
to
A_{K}
and
b
to
A^{\prime}_{K}
(resp.
a
to
A_{S}
and
b
to
A^{\prime}_{S}
) and replace any vertex
x\in S_{a}
and any vertex
y^{\prime}\in S_{b}
by respectively
x^{*}\in K_{x}\cap S^{*}_{a}
and
y^{\prime*}\in K_{y}\cap S^{*}_{b}
in order to get an
\ell
-pretemplate partition with one more principal path. So from now on we may assume that the length of
P
is neither
\ell-1
nor
\ell-2
.

Let us assume that there exist
x\in S_{a}\cap A_{K}
and
y^{\prime}\in S_{b}\cap A^{\prime}_{K}
and let
x^{*}\in K_{x}\cap S^{*}_{a}
and
y^{\prime*}\in K_{y}^{\prime}\cap S^{*}_{b}
. If
x=y
then
ax^{*}P^{*}_{x}y^{\prime*}bPa
is a hole, and hence
P
has length
\ell-1
, a contradiction. So we may now assume that
x\neq y
,
S_{a}\cap K_{y}=\varnothing
and
S_{b}\cap K_{x^{\prime}}=\varnothing
. Then because of the hole
ax^{*}yP^{*}_{y}y^{\prime*}bPa
the length of
P
is
\ell-2
, a contradiction again.

So from now on we may assume by symmetry that
S_{a}\cap A_{K}=\varnothing
. Then there exists
x^{*}\in K_{x}\cap S^{*}_{a}
for some
x\in B
. Assume that there exists
y^{\prime*}\in K_{y}^{\prime}\cap S^{*}_{b}
for some
y^{\prime}\in A_{K}.
In case
xy\in E(G)
then
P
has length
\ell-2
because of the hole
ax^{*}yP^{*}_{y}y^{\prime*}bPa
and we would get a contradiction. Hence
xy,aw\notin E(G)
. Now the hole
ax^{*}wyP^{*}_{y}y^{\prime*}bPa
implies that
P
has length
\ell-3
. Then any vertex
u\in A
which is adjacent to
x
, is adjacent to
y
: if
u\in A_{K}
by definition of an even template, if
u\in A_{S}
because else
ax^{*}uP_{u}u^{\prime}y^{\prime*}bPa
is hole of length
2\ell-1
. However, as any vertex in
B
,
x
has two non adjacent neighbors
r,s\in A
and so
G
contains a square
x^{*}rysx^{*}
, a contradiction. So from now on we may assume that
S_{b}\subseteq B^{\prime}
and there exists
y^{\prime*}\in K_{y^{\prime}}\cap S^{*}_{b}
for some
y^{\prime}\in B^{\prime}
. By definition,
x
has at least one neighbor
r
in
A_{S}
. If
y^{\prime}r^{\prime}\in E(G)
then because of the hole
ax^{*}rP_{r}r^{\prime}y^{\prime*}bPa
we get that
P
has length
\ell-2
, a contradiction. So
r^{\prime}y^{\prime}\notin E(G)
and then
y^{\prime}\neq w^{\prime}
,
bw^{\prime}\notin E(G)
and
P
should have length
\ell-3
, because of the cycle
ax^{*}rP_{r}r^{\prime}w^{\prime}y^{\prime*}bPa
. By symmetry, we also have
x\neq w
.

So, by setting
\mathbb{A}_{K}=A_{K}\cup\{x^{*}\},\mathbb{A}_{S}=A_{S},\mathbb{B}=\{w\},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{K}=A^{\prime}_{K}\cup\{y^{*}\},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{S}=A^{\prime}_{S},\mathbb{B}^{\prime}=\{w^{\prime}\}

we obtain an even pretemplate partition contained in
G
with
k+1
paths (the new path is
x^{*}aPby^{*}
of length
\ell-1
), a contradiction.

∎

9.6
End of the proof

We may now conclude the proof of Lemma
9.1
.
If
G\setminus F_{1}
is empty, then conclusion (
a
)
holds. If
G\setminus F_{1}
is non-empty and
G\setminus F_{2}
is empty, then
conclusion (
b
) holds. Otherwise, we consider a connected
component
D
of
G\setminus F_{2}
and apply Lemma
9.15
. We
then see that
G
has a clique cutset, so
conclusion (
c
) holds.

10
Proof of Theorem
10.1

Theorem 10.1

Let
\ell\geq 4
be an integer. If
G
is a graph in
\mathcal{C}_{2\ell}
then
one of the following holds:

(a)

G
is a ring of length
2\ell
;

(b)

G
is a proper blowup of a twinless even

\ell
-template;

(c)

G
has a universal vertex or

(d)

G
has a clique cutset.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.9
,
G
contains no unbalanced prism, no unbalanced theta, no pyramid and no
proper wheel. Also, clearly
G
contains no
C_{4}
and no
C_{5}
.
Hence, by Theorem
3.13
, we may assume that
G
contains a
prism or a theta for otherwise one of the conclusions (
a
),
(
c
) or (
d
) holds. The result then follows
from Lemma
9.1
.
∎
